denote a semisimple algebra that is isomorphic to the commutant algebra
\mathrm{End}_{G}(V^{\otimes t})
; for any
a\in\mathcal{A}_{t}
, let
S(a)\in\mathrm{End}_{G}(V^{\otimes t})
denote its action.

Finally, let
\mathsf{Schur}
be the corresponding generalized Schur transform. Let
V_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}^{\lambda}
and
V_{G}^{\lambda}
be the corresponding simple
\mathcal{A}_{t}
-modules and
G
-representations with bases
\mathcal{B}(V_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}^{\lambda})
and
\mathcal{B}(V_{G}^{\lambda})
implied by the Schur transform.

Then,

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{A}_{t})}S(\sigma)\otimes S(\sigma^{*})^{T}.

(5.30)

Proof.

We calculate

\displaystyle\left(\mathsf{Schur}\otimes\overline{\mathsf{Schur}}\right)\left(\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{A}_{t})}S(\sigma)\otimes S(\sigma^{*})^{T}\right)\left(\mathsf{Schur}^{\dagger}\otimes\mathsf{Schur}^{T}\right)

(5.31)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda,\mu,\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{t}\end{subarray}}\outerproduct*{\lambda, \mu}{\lambda, \mu}\otimes\left(\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{A}_{t})}\rho_{\lambda}(\sigma)\otimes\rho_{\mu}(\sigma^{*})^{T}\right)\otimes\mathbf{I}_{V_{G}^{\lambda}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{V_{G}^{\mu}}

(definition of Schur transform (
Definition

3.22
))

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda,\mu,\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{t}\end{subarray}}\outerproduct*{\lambda, \mu}{\lambda, \mu}\otimes\left(\sum_{\ket{a},\ket{b}\ket{c},\ket{d},\in\mathcal{B}(V_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}^{\lambda})}\outerproduct{a}{b}\otimes\outerproduct{d}{c}\right)\otimes\mathbf{I}_{V_{G}^{\lambda}\otimes V_{G}^{\mu}}\cdot\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{A}_{t})}\bra{a}\rho_{\lambda}(\sigma)\ket{b}\cdot\bra{d}\rho_{\mu}(\sigma^{*})^{T}\ket{c}

(
\sum_{a}\outerproduct*{a}{a}=\mathbf{I}
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda,\mu,\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{t}\end{subarray}}\outerproduct*{\lambda, \mu}{\lambda, \mu}\otimes\left(\sum_{\ket{a},\ket{b}\ket{c},\ket{d},\in\mathcal{B}(V_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}^{\lambda})}\outerproduct{a}{b}\otimes\outerproduct{d}{c}\right)\otimes\mathbf{I}_{V_{G}^{\lambda}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{V_{G}^{\mu}}\cdot\frac{1}{\dim(V_{\lambda})}\delta_{\lambda\mu}\delta_{ad}\delta_{bc}

(
Theorem

5.9
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{t}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\dim(V_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}^{\lambda})}\outerproduct*{\lambda, \lambda}{\lambda, \lambda}\otimes\left(\sum_{\ket{a},\ket{b}\in\mathcal{B}(V_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}^{\lambda})}\outerproduct{a}{b}\otimes\outerproduct{a}{b}\right)\otimes\mathbf{I}_{V_{G}^{\lambda}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{V_{G}^{\mu}}

(5.32)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{t}\\
\ket{a},\ket{b}\in\mathcal{B}(V_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}^{\lambda})\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\dim(V_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}^{\lambda})}\outerproduct*{\lambda, \lambda}{\lambda, \lambda}\otimes\outerproduct{
a, a
}{
b, b
}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{V_{G}^{\lambda}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{V_{G}^{\mu}}

(5.33)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathsf{Schur}\otimes\overline{\mathsf{Schur}}\right)\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}\left(\mathsf{Schur}^{\dagger}\otimes\mathsf{Schur}^{T}\right)

∎

Example 5.12

(Group Algebra Commutant EPR Projector)
.

In the case of
\mathcal{A}_{t}=\mathbb{C}[H]
for some finite group
H
, we have that
[h]^{*}=\frac{1}{|H|}[h^{-1}]
for all
h\in H
(
Example

3.18
); since
S([h^{-1}])^{T}=\overline{S([h])}
for all
h
, this means that the commutant EPR projector formula simplifies to

\Omega_{\mathbb{C}[H]}=\frac{1}{|H|}\sum_{h\in H}S([h])\otimes\overline{S([h])}.

This further simplifies to

\Omega_{\mathbb{C}[H]}=\frac{1}{|H|}\sum_{h\in H}S([h])\otimes S([h])

whenever the Schur representation
S(\cdot)
has real entries, such as in the case of
H=S_{t}
.

5.9
Adaptive Queries to the Update Rule

In this section, we derive an equivalent form for the general update rule in
Section

5.1
, which we will use for the main argument in
Section

7.5
. Specifically, we are interested in analyzing (potentially adaptive) quantum query algorithms that have oracle access to one of two different isometries:

•

The path basis update
Q_{\rho}^{\mathrm{Path}}
, or

•

Q_{\rho}^{\mathrm{Path}}\cdot C
, which applies
Q_{\rho}^{\mathrm{Path}}
after first applying a unitary
C
to the adversary’s query register.

We will use
\mathsf{A}
,
\mathsf{B}
, and
\mathsf{R}
to denote the query, workspace, and database registers as defined in
Definition

3.5
. We will also subdivide
\mathsf{R}
as

\mathsf{R}=(\mathsf{R_{0}},\mathsf{R_{1}}=(\mathsf{R_{X,1},R_{Y,1}}),\mathsf{R_{2}}=(\mathsf{R_{X,2},R_{Y,2}}),\dots,\mathsf{R_{t}}=(\mathsf{R_{X,t},R_{Y,t}}))

(5.34)

where
\mathsf{R_{i}}
denotes the register to which the update operator adds the
i
th query, and
\mathsf{R_{X,i}}
and
\mathsf{R_{Y,i}}
denote the registers for recording the input and output for the
i
’th query. We also write
\mathsf{R_{X}}:=(\mathsf{R_{X,1}},\mathsf{R_{X,2}},\dots\mathsf{R_{X,t}})
and
\mathsf{R_{Y}}:=(\mathsf{R_{Y,1}},\mathsf{R_{Y,2}},\dots\mathsf{R_{Y,t}})
.
We will also use
\mathsf{R_{0}}
to denote register containing the initial database, before making any queries (which we will assume to be properly formatted as a list of pairs of registers like the rest of
\mathsf{R}
). Most often, we will take
\mathsf{R_{0}}
to be the empty (
0
qubit) register (with associated Hilbert space
\mathbb{C}^{2^{0}}=\mathbb{C}
). We write such an empty register as
\ket{\emptyset}_{\mathsf{R}}
. However it can be non-empty if, for example, we are not starting with a clean slate and a previous algorithm has already made queries.
We will also write
\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}:=(\mathsf{R}_{0},\mathsf{R}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{R}_{i})
.

We will use
\mathcal{A}_{i}
to denote the commutant algebra of the
k
’th tensor product of the group representation, where
k=|\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}|=i+|\mathsf{R}_{0}|
(the size of
\mathsf{R}_{0}
being the number of pairs in the initial database).
When the database is empty, then this is the commutant algebra of the trivial representation of the group, which is simply the one-dimensional algebra
\mathbb{C}
.

Notation for the Update Rule

We will frequently drop the subscript
t
from the Schur transform
\mathsf{Schur}_{t}
when the tensor power is unambiguous. For ease of working with the update rule from
Section

5.1
, we will write it in terms of the following operators:

Let

\displaystyle\Pi_{\lambda,i}

\displaystyle:=\mathsf{Schur}^{\dagger}\Big(\outerproduct*{\lambda}{\lambda}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{V_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}^{\lambda}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{V_{G}^{\lambda}}\Big)\mathsf{Schur}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{\overline{V}^{\otimes t}}\,,

(5.35)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\lambda,i}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\beta_{\lambda,i}\;\Pi_{\lambda,i}\,,\qquad\Lambda_{\lambda,i}^{+}\coloneqq\beta_{\lambda,i}^{-1}\;\Pi_{\lambda,i}\,,\qquad\text{where }\beta_{\lambda,i}\coloneqq\sqrt{\frac{\dim(V^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{A}_{i}})}{\dim(V^{\lambda}_{G})}}\,,

(5.36)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{\lambda\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{i}}\Lambda_{\lambda,i}\,,\qquad\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}^{+}\coloneqq\sum_{\lambda\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{i}}\Lambda_{\lambda,i}^{+}

(5.37)

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}

\displaystyle:=\left(\mathsf{Schur}^{\dagger}\otimes\mathsf{Schur}^{T}\right)\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{i}\\
\ket{a},\ket{b}\in\mathcal{B}(V_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}^{\lambda})\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\dim(V_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}^{\lambda})}\outerproduct*{\lambda, \lambda}{\lambda, \lambda}\otimes\outerproduct{
a, a
}{
b, b
}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{V_{G}^{\lambda}}^{\otimes 2}\right)\left(\mathsf{Schur}\otimes\overline{\mathsf{Schur}}\right)

(5.38)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{A}_{i})}S(\sigma)\otimes S(\sigma^{*})^{T}\,.

(by
Lemma

5.11
, where
S
is the Schur representation)

\displaystyle\operatorname{App}_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{x,y\in[N]}\left(\outerproduct{y}{x}\right)_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\ket{x, y}_{\mathsf{R_{i}}}

(5.39)

When the database register is of size
i-1
, we can write the path recording update rule (
Remark

5.2
), when restricted to input databases in
\im(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}})
, as

\displaystyle Q_{\rho}^{\mathrm{Path}}=V:=\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\;\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i-1}}

(5.40)

Rewriting the Update Operator for Adaptive Queries

Lemma 5.13

(Telescoping product of irrep ratio reweighting)
.

Let
V
be the isometry defined in
Eq.

5.40
, and define the reduced update operator (which without the rescaling is not an isometry) to be

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}

\displaystyle:=\Big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{i}}

(5.41)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{i},y_{i}\in[N]}\outerproduct{y_i}{x_i}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big(\mathbf{I}_{\mathsf{R}_{<i}}\otimes\ket{x_i, y_i}_{\mathsf{R}_{i}}\Big)\,.

(5.42)

whenever the database register
\mathsf{R}
is of size
i-1
.
Let
\mathsf{Adv}_{t}
be any
t
-query algorithm with workspace register
\mathsf{A}
, ancilla register
\mathsf{B}
, and that makes queries to the update rule
V
on workspace register
\mathsf{A}
and database register
\mathsf{R}
. Then

\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V}=\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq t}}\;\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\widetilde{V}}\;\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{0}}

(5.43)

In other words, if we make
t
queries to the path recording oracle, then this is the same as if we were to apply the path recording update rule without the reweighting, and then apply a reweighting just once at the end.
44
44

44

As described in
Remark

5.19
, when beginning with an empty database, the first reweighting operator always occurs on the one-dimensional irreps corresponding to
|\lambda|=0
, and is thus just the identity operator.

Proof.

Let
(A_{1})_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}},\dots,(A_{t})_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}
be the unitaries that
\mathsf{Adv}
applies in between queries.
We can write

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V}

(5.44)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(V_{\mathsf{AR}}\cdot A_{i\,\mathsf{AB}}\right)

(5.45)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{i}}\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i-1}}\big(A_{i}\big)_{\mathsf{AB}}\right)

(by the definition of
V
)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\left(\sum_{\lambda^{+}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{i}}\beta_{\lambda^{+},i}\;\Pi_{\lambda^{+},i}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{i}}\left(\sum_{\lambda\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{i-1}}\beta_{\lambda,i-1}^{-1}\;\Pi_{\lambda,i-1}\right)_{\mathsf{R_{\leq i-1}}}\big(A_{i}\big)_{\mathsf{AB}}\right)

(by the definition of
\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{t}\\
\lambda^{+}_{1},\dots,\lambda^{+}_{t}\\
\lambda_{i}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{i-1},\;\lambda^{+}_{i}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{i}\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\left(\beta_{\lambda^{+}_{i},i}\;\Pi_{\lambda^{+}_{i},i}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{i}}\left(\beta_{\lambda_{i},i-1}^{-1}\;\Pi_{\lambda_{i},i-1}\right)_{\mathsf{R_{\leq i-1}}}\big(A_{i}\big)_{\mathsf{AB}}\right)

(5.46)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{t}\\
\lambda^{+}_{1},\dots,\lambda^{+}_{t}\\
\lambda_{i}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{i-1},\;\lambda^{+}_{i}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{i}\end{subarray}}\beta_{\lambda^{+}_{t},t}\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\left(\beta_{\lambda_{i+1},i}^{-1}\;\Pi_{\lambda_{i+1},i}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\left(\beta_{\lambda^{+}_{i},i}\;\Pi_{\lambda^{+}_{i},i}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{i}}\big(A_{i}\big)_{\mathsf{AB}}\right)\left(\beta_{\lambda_{1},0}^{-1}\;\Pi_{\lambda_{1},0}\right)_{\mathsf{R_{0}}}

(by moving each

\beta_{\lambda_{i},i-1}^{-1}\;\Pi_{\lambda_{i},i-1}
to the previous factor in the product
45
45
footnotemark:

45

)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{t}\\
\lambda^{+}_{1},\dots,\lambda^{+}_{t}\\
\lambda_{i}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{i-1},\;\lambda^{+}_{i}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{i}\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\frac{\beta_{\lambda^{+}_{i},i}}{\beta_{\lambda_{i+1},i}}\;\left(\Pi_{\lambda_{i+1},i}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\left(\Pi_{\lambda^{+}_{i},i}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{i}}\big(A_{i}\big)_{\mathsf{AB}}\right)\left(\Pi_{\lambda_{1},0}\right)_{\mathsf{R_{0}}}\frac{\beta_{\lambda^{+}_{t},t}}{\beta_{\lambda_{1},0}}

(5.47)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{t}\\
\lambda^{+}_{1},\dots,\lambda^{+}_{t}\\
\lambda_{i}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{i-1},\;\lambda^{+}_{i}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{i}\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\frac{\beta_{\lambda^{+}_{i},i}}{\beta_{\lambda_{i+1},i}}\;\delta_{\lambda_{i+1},\,\lambda_{i}^{+}}\left(\Pi_{\lambda^{+}_{i},i}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{i}}\big(A_{i}\big)_{\mathsf{AB}}\right)\left(\Pi_{\lambda_{1},0}\right)_{\mathsf{R_{0}}}\frac{\beta_{\lambda^{+}_{t},t}}{\beta_{\lambda_{1},0}}

\left(\text{since}\;\;\Pi_{\lambda_{i+1}}\Pi_{\lambda^{+}_{i}}=\delta_{\lambda_{i+1},\lambda^{+}_{i}}\;\Pi_{\lambda^{+}_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda_{1},\lambda^{+}_{1},\dots,\lambda^{+}_{t}\\
\lambda_{1}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{0},\;\lambda^{+}_{i}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{i}\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\left(\Pi_{\lambda^{+}_{i},i}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{i}}\big(A_{i}\big)_{\mathsf{AB}}\right)\left(\Pi_{\lambda_{1},0}\right)_{\mathsf{R_{0}}}\frac{\beta_{\lambda^{+}_{t},t}}{\beta_{\lambda_{1},0}}

(5.48)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda^{+}_{i}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{i}\end{subarray}}\Pi_{\lambda^{+}_{i},i}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{i}}\big(A_{i}\big)_{\mathsf{AB}}\right)\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda_{1}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{0}\end{subarray}}\Pi_{\lambda_{1},0}\right)_{\mathsf{R_{0}}}\frac{\beta_{\lambda^{+}_{t},t}}{\beta_{\lambda_{1},0}}

(5.49)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda^{+}_{t}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{t}\end{subarray}}\beta_{\lambda^{+}_{t},t}\;\Pi_{\lambda^{+}_{t},t}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq t}}\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\Big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{i}}\big(A_{i}\big)_{\mathsf{AB}}\right)\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda_{1}\in\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{0}\end{subarray}}\beta_{\lambda_{1},0}^{-1}\;\Pi_{\lambda_{1},0}\right)_{\mathsf{R_{0}}}

(Using that
\sum_{\lambda}\Pi_{\lambda}=\mathbf{I}
in all except the last factor of the product)

\displaystyle=\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq t}}\;\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\widetilde{V}}\;\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{0}}

(by the definitions of
\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}
,
\widetilde{V}
)

∎

Lemma 5.14
.

The same holds for queries to
V\cdot C
for any unitary
C\in U(N)
acting on the
\mathsf{A}
register. That is,

\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V\cdot C}=\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq t}}\;\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\widetilde{V}\cdot C}\;\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{0}}\,.

(5.50)

The proof is the same as that of
Lemma

5.13
above, but where each
A_{i\,\mathsf{AB}}
has an extra
C
attached to the left.

Lemma 5.15

(Ricochet Property of the Append Operator)
.

For any
N
-dimensional unitary
C\in U(N)
,

\operatorname{App}_{\mathsf{A\,R_{i}}}\cdot\;C_{\mathsf{A}}=C_{{\mathsf{R_{X,i}}}}\cdot\operatorname{App}_{\mathsf{A\,R_{i}}}

(5.51)

Proof.

\displaystyle\operatorname{App}_{\mathsf{A\,R_{i}}}\cdot\;C_{\mathsf{A}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{x,y\in[N]}\outerproduct{y}{x}_{\mathsf{A}}C_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\ket{x, y}_{\mathsf{R}_{i}}

(Definition of
\operatorname{App}_{\mathsf{A\,R_{i}}}
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x,y,z\in[N]}\ket{y}_{\mathsf{A}}\langle x|C|z\rangle\bra{z}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\ket{x, y}_{\mathsf{R}_{i}}

(
\sum_{z}\outerproduct{z}{z}=\mathbf{I}
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{y,z\in[N]}\outerproduct{y}{z}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\sum_{x\in[N]}\langle x|C|z\rangle\ket{x, y}_{\mathsf{R}_{i}}

(5.52)

\displaystyle=\sum_{y,z\in[N]}\outerproduct{y}{z}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\sum_{x\in[N]}\ket{x}_{\mathsf{R_{X,i}}}\langle x|C|z\rangle\ket{y}_{\mathsf{R_{Y,i}}}

(5.53)

\displaystyle=\sum_{y,z\in[N]}\outerproduct{y}{z}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes C\ket{z}_{\mathsf{R_{X,i}}}\otimes\ket{y}_{\mathsf{R_{Y,i}}}

(
\sum_{x}\outerproduct{x}{x}=\mathbf{I}
)

\displaystyle=C_{{\mathsf{R_{X,i}}}}\cdot\operatorname{App}_{\mathsf{A\,R_{i}}}

∎

Lemma 5.16

(Alternate Form of
\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\widetilde{V}\cdot C}
)
.

\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\widetilde{V}\cdot C}=\left(\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\right)\cdot\left(C^{\otimes t}\right)_{\mathsf{R_{X}}}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}

(5.54)

In other words, the following process is identical to querying
\widetilde{V}\cdot C
: during each query, only the
\operatorname{App}
operator is applied. Then, after all queries have been made,
C^{\otimes t}
is applied to
\mathsf{R_{X}}
, the input half of the database in the oracle register, and then for every
i\in[t]
,
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}
is applied to the first
i
registers of
\mathsf{R}
.

Proof.

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\widetilde{V}\cdot C}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\widetilde{V}_{\mathsf{A\,R}}\cdot C_{\mathsf{A}}\cdot A_{i\,\mathsf{AB}}\right)

(5.55)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\cdot\operatorname{App}_{\mathsf{A\,R_{i}}}\cdot C_{\mathsf{A}}\cdot A_{i\,\mathsf{AB}}\right)

(Definition of
\widetilde{V}
)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\cdot C_{\mathsf{R_{X,i}}}\cdot\operatorname{App}_{\mathsf{A\,R_{i}}}\cdot A_{i\,\mathsf{AB}}\right)

(
Lemma

5.15
)

Next, notice that for all
j<i
,
\operatorname{App}_{\mathsf{A\,R_{i}}}\cdot A_{i\,\mathsf{AB}}
commutes with both
C_{\mathsf{R_{X,j}}}
and
\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{j}}\right)_{\mathsf{R_{\leq j}}}
, since the former acts only on the
\mathsf{A}
,
\mathsf{B}
, and
\mathsf{R_{i}}
registers, while both of the latter operators act only on
(\mathsf{R_{1}}\dots\mathsf{R_{j}})
. Therefore, we can commute them all past:

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\cdot C_{\mathsf{R_{X,i}}}\right)\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\operatorname{App}_{\mathsf{A\,R_{i}}}\cdot A_{i\,\mathsf{AB}}\right)

(5.56)

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\right)\cdot\left(\prod_{i=1}^{t}C_{\mathsf{R_{X,i}}}\right)\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\operatorname{App}_{\mathsf{A\,R_{i}}}\cdot A_{i\,\mathsf{AB}}\right)

(
C_{\mathsf{R_{X,i}}}
similarly commutes with
\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{j}}\right)_{\mathsf{R_{\leq j}}}
, for all
j<i
)

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\right)\cdot\Big(C^{\otimes t}\Big)_{\mathsf{R_{X}}}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\qed

(5.57)

Lemma 5.17
.

\displaystyle\prod_{k=1}^{t}\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{k}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq k}}=\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}

(5.58)

Proof.

For each
k
, write
S_{k}:\mathcal{A}_{k}\to\mathrm{End}(V^{\otimes k})
for the Schur
representation action of
\mathcal{A}_{k}
on the first
k
path registers. When

k\leq t
, we view
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{k}}
as an operator on
\mathsf{R}_{\leq t}
by
tensoring with
\mathbf{I}
on the later registers
\mathsf{R}_{k+1},\ldots,\mathsf{R}_{t}
.

We first recall the subspace onto which
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{k}}
projects. Namely,

\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{k}}
is the orthogonal projector onto the subspace of states

\ket{\psi}
satisfying

\bigl(S_{k}(a)\bigr)_{\mathsf{X}_{\leq k}}\ket{\psi}=\bigl(S_{k}(a)^{T}\bigr)_{\mathsf{Y}_{\leq k}}\ket{\psi}\qquad\forall a\in\mathcal{A}_{k}.

Here the
\mathsf{X}_{\leq k}
registers are identified with
V^{\otimes k}
,
whereas the
\mathsf{Y}_{\leq k}
registers are identified with

\overline{V}^{\otimes k}
, using the conjugate basis labeled by the same
basis elements.

To see the characterization, pass to the double Schur basis for

V^{\otimes k}\otimes\overline{V}^{\otimes k}
. In this basis,

V^{\otimes k}\otimes\overline{V}^{\otimes k}\cong\bigoplus_{\lambda,\mu}\left(V_{G}^{\lambda}\otimes V_{\mathcal{A}_{k}}^{\lambda}\right)\otimes\left(\overline{V_{G}^{\mu}}\otimes\overline{V_{\mathcal{A}_{k}}^{\mu}}\right).

The algebra
\mathcal{A}_{k}
acts on the
\mathsf{X}
half by

S_{k}(a)=\bigoplus_{\lambda}\mathbf{I}_{V_{G}^{\lambda}}\otimes\rho_{\lambda}(a),

and the transposed action on the
\mathsf{Y}
half is

S_{k}(a)^{T}=\bigoplus_{\mu}\mathbf{I}_{\overline{V_{G}^{\mu}}}\otimes\rho_{\mu}(a)^{T}.

Taking
a
to be central idempotents of
\mathcal{A}_{k}
first forces any state
satisfying the displayed relations to have support only on the

(\lambda,\lambda)
blocks. On such a block, after treating the two

G
-registers as spectators, the relations become

\bigl(\rho_{\lambda}(a)\otimes\mathbf{I}\bigr)\ket{\psi_{\lambda,\lambda}}=\bigl(\mathbf{I}\otimes\rho_{\lambda}(a)^{T}\bigr)\ket{\psi_{\lambda,\lambda}}\qquad\forall a\in\mathcal{A}_{k}.

Since
\rho_{\lambda}(\mathcal{A}_{k})=\mathrm{End}(V_{\mathcal{A}_{k}}^{\lambda})
, this is equivalent to

(M\otimes\mathbf{I})\ket{\psi_{\lambda,\lambda}}=(\mathbf{I}\otimes M^{T})\ket{\psi_{\lambda,\lambda}}\qquad\forall M\in\mathrm{End}(V_{\mathcal{A}_{k}}^{\lambda}).

The solutions are precisely arbitrary states on the two
G
-registers tensored
with the EPR state on the two commutant registers,

\ket{\mathsf{EPR}_{\calA_k}^{\lambda}}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\dim(V_{\mathcal{A}_{k}}^{\lambda})}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{B}(V_{\mathcal{A}_{k}}^{\lambda})}\ket{T}\otimes\ket{T},

where the second
\ket{T}
denotes the corresponding conjugate-basis vector
in
\overline{V_{\mathcal{A}_{k}}^{\lambda}}
. This is exactly the image of

\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{k}}
by definition.

The lemma now follows from the fact that the
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{k}}
are all orthogonal projectors onto subspaces that form a linear chain. This is because the commutant algebras satisfy
S(\mathcal{A}_{k})\subset S(\mathcal{A}_{t})
for all
k\leq t
, so the constraints

\bigl(S_{k}(a)\bigr)_{\mathsf{X}_{\leq k}}\ket{\psi}=\bigl(S_{k}(a)^{T}\bigr)_{\mathsf{Y}_{\leq k}}\ket{\psi}\qquad\forall a\in\mathcal{A}_{k}

are contained within the corresponding set of constraints for
\mathcal{A}_{t}
. This implies that

\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{k}}=\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}

for all
k\leq t
, which proves the lemma.
∎

Combining
Lemmas

5.16
,
5.14
and
5.17
, we obtain an equivalent expanded form for an adversary querying
V\cdot C
:

Corollary 5.18
.

\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V\cdot C}=\underbrace{\vphantom{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\right)}\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq t}}}_{\operatorname{ReweightEnd}}\cdot\underbrace{\vphantom{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\right)}\Big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq t}}}_{\operatorname{Symmetrize}}\cdot\underbrace{\vphantom{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\right)}\Big(C^{\otimes t}\Big)_{\mathsf{R_{X}}}}_{C^{\otimes t}}\cdot\underbrace{\vphantom{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\right)}\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}}_{\operatorname{Append}}\cdot\underbrace{\vphantom{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\right)}\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{0}}}_{\operatorname{ReweightStart}}

(5.59)

Both reweighting steps, as well as the
\operatorname{Symmetrize}
step, depend on the group
G
(in particular, they depend on the commutant algebra of its tensor power representation), while
C^{\otimes t}
and
\operatorname{Append}
are independent of the group and its representation.

When we start with
\mathsf{R_{0}}
containing the empty database, this simplifies further, since initial reweighting step disappears:

Remark 5.19
.

When
R
contains the empty database,
\ket{\emptyset}
, then

\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{0}}^{+}\ket{\emptyset}_{\mathsf{R}}=\ket{\emptyset}_{\mathsf{R}}\,.

Proof.

Since the database register
\mathsf{R}
is empty, it corresponds to the 1-dimensional space
(\mathbb{C}^{N})^{\otimes 0}=\mathbb{C}
, on which
G
acts via the trivial irrep (
\rho^{\otimes 0}=1
), and its commutant
\mathcal{A}_{0}=\mathbb{C}
is a simple module (its only irrep is the one that acts via scalar multiplication). Since the irreps are both 1-dimensional, the ratio
\beta_{\lambda}
is 1, and the rescaling has no effect.
∎

6
Approximation and Specialization to Specific Groups

Our general update rule can recover an interpretable recording oracle for any group representation. To demonstrate its generality, in this section, we show how it recovers existing recording oracles, including Zhandry’s original recording oracle for functions
[
Zha19
]
and Ma and Huang’s path-recording oracle
[
MH25
]
for unitaries as special cases.
For the latter, since the path recording oracle of
[
MH25
]
is only approximate, we describe two explicit approximations that relate our path-recording oracle to theirs.

We also analyze the
unitary Haar cipher
model, in which unitaries
U_{1},\,\dots,\,U_{K}
are each chosen i.i.d. from the Haar measure on
U(N)
and queried in a controlled manner as
\sum_{k\in[K]}\outerproduct*{k}{k}\otimes U_{k}
.

6.1
The Unitary Path-Recording Oracle of
[
MH25
]

We will use the following lemma from
[
MH25
]
:

Lemma 6.1

(
[
MH25
, Lemma 2.2]
)
.

Let
\rho_{\mathsf{ST}}
be a density matrix on registers
\mathsf{S},\mathsf{T}
and let
\Pi_{\mathsf{T}}
be a projector that acts on register
\mathsf{T}
. Then

\left\|\operatorname{Tr}_{\mathsf{T}}(\rho_{\mathsf{ST}})-\operatorname{Tr}_{\mathsf{T}}(\Pi_{\mathsf{T}}\rho_{\mathsf{ST}}\Pi_{\mathsf{T}})\right\|_{1}=1-\operatorname{Tr}(\Pi_{\mathsf{T}}\rho_{\mathsf{{ST}}}).

(6.1)

6.1.1
The Distinct Subspace

Definition 6.2
.

For
1\leq t\leq N
, define

[N]_{\text{dist}}^{t}\coloneqq\{(x_{1},\dots,x_{t})\in[N]^{t}:\text{all $x_{i}$'s distinct}\}

(6.2)

as the set of all distinct ordered
t
-tuples over
[N]
.

Definition 6.3
.

The
distinct subspace

\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}\subseteq(\mathbb{C}^{N})^{\otimes t}
is defined as the span of all distinct strings, i.e.

\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}\coloneqq\text{span}\{\ket{x}:x\in[N]_{\text{dist}}^{t}\}

(6.3)

6.1.2
The
[
MH25
]
oracle

The
[
MH25
]
path-recording oracle acts on an adversary input register
\mathsf{A}
and recording register
\mathsf{R}_{MH}
whose basis consists of
sets
of input-output pairs
\{(x_{1},y_{1}),\ldots,(x_{t},y_{t})\}
with the property that
(y_{1},\ldots,y_{t})
are distinct.

To compare this with our compressed oracle, we consider the following simple isometry mapping
\mathsf{R}_{MH}
to our recording space
\mathsf{R}
:

\ket{\{(x_1, y_1), \hdots, (x_t, y_t)\}}\mapsto\frac{1}{\sqrt{t!}}\sum_{\pi\in S_{t}}\ket{x_{\pi^{-1}(1)}, \hdots, x_{\pi^{-1}(t)}, y_{\pi^{-1}(1)}, \hdots, y_{\pi^{-1}(t)}}=\sqrt{t!}\cdot\Omega_{S_{t}}\ket{x_1, \hdots, x_t, y_1, \hdots, y_t}.

Via this isometry, we identify the Ma-Huang path recording update with the following update rule

\displaystyle\ket{x_{t}}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\ket{x_1, \hdots, x_{t-1}, y_1, \hdots, y_{t-1}}

\displaystyle\mapsto\Omega_{S_{t}}\cdot\frac{\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{N-t+1}}\sum_{y_{t}\notin\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{t-1}\}}\ket{y_{t}}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\ket{x_1, \hdots, x_{t}, y_1, \hdots, y_{t}}

(6.4)

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{t}}{\sqrt{N-t+1}}\cdot\Big(\Omega_{S_{t}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}}\cdot\Big(\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{Y}}\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}}.

(6.5)

Thus, the statement that the Ma-Huang oracle is indistinguishable from our oracle amounts to two informal statements:

•

The application of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}
is approximately the identity map, and

•

The overall effect of inverse subspace reweighting
\Lambda_{S_{t-1}}^{+}
and reweighting
\Lambda_{S_{t}}
is approximately constant, with a scalar factor of
\sqrt{\frac{t}{N-t+1}}
.

Next, we will give a formal justification for the approximate equivalence of
V_{MH}
and
V_{U(N)}
for
t
-query quantum algorithms.

6.1.3
Proof of approximate equivalence

Using the fact that
(\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}})_{\mathsf{R}_{Y,\leq t}}
commutes with
\Omega_{S_{t}}
, we can then express the isometry implemented by a
t
-query adaptive adversary
\mathsf{Adv}^{V_{MH}}
as

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{MH}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{t!}\prod_{i=1}^{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-i+1}}\cdot\Big(\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{Y}}\cdot\Big(\Omega_{S_{t}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}

(6.6)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{{N\choose t}}}\cdot\Big(\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{Y}}\cdot\Big(\Omega_{S_{t}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\,.

(6.7)

On the other hand, our own path-recording oracle implements

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}}

\displaystyle=\Big(\Lambda_{S_{t}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}}\cdot\Big(\Omega_{S_{t}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\cdot\Big(\Lambda_{S_{0}}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{0}}

(6.8)

\displaystyle=\Big(\Lambda_{S_{t}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}}\cdot\Big(\Omega_{S_{t}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}.

(6.9)

To compare these, define the quantum states

\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{MH}}}=\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{MH}}\ket{0}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}\ket{\emptyset}_{\mathsf{R}},\qquad\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)}}}=\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}}\ket{0}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}\ket{\emptyset}_{\mathsf{R}}.

(6.10)

We wish to derive the fact that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}^{\phantom{R}}\!\!\left[\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{MH}}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{MH}}}\right\rvert\right]\!-\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}^{\phantom{R}}\!\!\left[\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}}}\right\rvert\right]\right\rVert_{1}\leq O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)

(6.11)

We prove this via the following sequence of steps. First, we show that

\displaystyle\Big|\Big|(\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}})_{\mathsf{R}_{Y}}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)}}}\Big|\Big|^{2}\geq 1-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right).

(6.12)

Then, by
Lemma

6.1
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}^{\phantom{R}}\!\!\left[(\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}})_{\mathsf{R}_{Y}}\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}}}\right\rvert(\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}})_{\mathsf{R}_{Y}}\right]\!-\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}^{\phantom{R}}\!\!\left[\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}}}\right\rvert\right]\right\rVert_{1}\leq O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)

(6.13)

Finally, we prove that

\displaystyle\left\lVert(\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}})_{\mathsf{R}_{Y}}\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}}}\right\rvert(\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}})_{\mathsf{R}_{Y}}\!-\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{MH}}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{MH}}}\right\rvert\right\rVert_{1}\leq O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)

(6.14)

For the remainder of this subsection, write

\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}:=\left(\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{Y}},\qquad\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist},t-1}^{Y}:=\left(\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t-1}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{Y,<t}},\qquad\alpha_{t}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\binom{N}{t}}}.

We also abbreviate the append-only state by

\ket{\psi_t}:=\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\ket{0}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}\ket{\emptyset}_{\mathsf{R}}.

With this notation,
Eq.

6.6
says

\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{MH}}}=\alpha_{t}\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\cdot\Omega_{S_{t}}\ket{\psi_t}.

while
Eq.

6.9
says

\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)}}}=\Lambda_{S_{t}}\cdot\Omega_{S_{t}}\ket{\psi_t}.

Lemma 6.4

(Analyzing
\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}
)
.

For every non-identity
\pi\in S_{t}
,

\bra{\psi_t}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\Big(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi)\Big)\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}=0.

Moreover,

\bra{\psi_t}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}=t!\binom{N}{t}.

Consequently,

\left\|\Omega_{S_{t}}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}\right\|^{2}=\binom{N}{t}=\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}^{2}}.

Proof.

We prove the first two claims by induction on
t
. The case
t=1
is trivial (there are no non-identity permutations and
\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}=\mathbf{I}
).

For the inductive step, we write

\ket{\psi_t}=\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{t}}\cdot(A_{t})_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}\cdot\ket{\psi}_{t-1}=\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{t}}\ket{\psi_t^{\mathrm{pre}}}.

Moreover, since
\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}=\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist},t-1}^{Y}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist},t-1}^{Y}
commutes with
\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{t}}
, we have that

\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}=\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\cdot\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{t}}\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist},t-1}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t^{\mathrm{pre}}}.

We now write explicitly in the standard basis

\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist},t-1}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t^{\mathrm{pre}}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}\\
\text{distinct }y_{1},\ldots,y_{t-1}\end{subarray}}\ket{x_t}_{\mathsf{A}}\ket{\phi_{x_{1:t}, y_{1:t-1}}}_{\mathsf{B}}\ket{x_1,\hdots x_{t-1}, y_1, \hdots, y_{t-1}}_{\mathsf{R}_{<t}}.

On this state, the effect of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\cdot\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{t}}
is appending with respect to an (unnormalized) EPR state over
y_{t}\notin\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{t-1}\}
(controlled on
\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{t-1}\}
), which tells us that

\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}\\
\text{distinct }y_{1},\ldots,y_{t}\end{subarray}}\ket{y_t}_{\mathsf{A}}\ket{\phi_{x_{1:t}, y_{1:t-1}}}_{\mathsf{B}}\ket{x_1,\hdots x_{t}, y_1, \hdots, y_{t}}_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq t}}.

Given that
\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist},t-1}^{Y}
also commutes with
(A_{t})_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}
, this immediately tells us that

\Big|\Big|\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}\Big|\Big|^{2}=(N-t+1)\Big|\Big|\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist},t-1}^{Y}\ket{\psi_{t-1}}\Big|\Big|^{2},

completing the second part of the induction. For the first part, we have that

\Big(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi)\Big)\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}\\
\text{distinct }y_{1},\ldots,y_{t}\end{subarray}}\ket{y_t}_{\mathsf{A}}\ket{\phi_{x_{1:t}, y_{1:t-1}}}_{\mathsf{B}}\ket{x_{\pi^{-1}(1)},\hdots x_{\pi^{-1}(t)}, y_{\pi^{-1}(1)}, \hdots, y_{\pi^{-1}(t)}}_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq t}}.

Now, suppose that
\pi(t)\neq t
. Then, the inner product of these two states is zero because a nonzero term in the expansion of this inner product would require terms
y_{t}=y^{\prime}_{t}
on the
\mathsf{A}
register but also
y_{t}=y^{\prime}_{\pi^{-1}(t)}
on the
\mathsf{R}_{Y,t}
register.

On the other hand, suppose that
\pi(t)=t
and identify
\pi
as a non-identity element of
S_{t-1}
; in particular,
S(\pi)
commutes with
\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{t}}\cdot(A_{t})_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}
. Then, we write

\bra{\psi_t}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\Big(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi)\Big)\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}=\bra{\psi_{t}^{\mathrm{pre}}}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist},t-1}^{Y}\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{R}_{t}}^{\dagger}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{R}_{t}}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist},t-1}^{Y}\Big(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi)\Big)\ket{\psi_{t}^{\mathrm{pre}}}

Next, we make use of the identity

\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist},t-1}^{Y}\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{R}_{t}}^{\dagger}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{R}_{t}}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist},t-1}^{Y}=(N-t+1)\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist},t-1}^{Y},

which again holds because the effect of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{R}_{t}}
on the image of
\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist},t-1}^{Y}
is to append an un-normalized EPR state over
y_{t}\notin\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{t-1}\}
:

\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{R}_{t}}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist},t-1}^{Y}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots x_{t}\\
\text{distinct }y_{1},\ldots,y_{t}\end{subarray}}\outerproduct{y_t}{x_t}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\outerproduct{x_1, \hdots, x_t, y_1, \hdots, y_t}{x_1, \hdots, x_{t-1}, y_1, \hdots, y_{t-1}}.

The claimed identity follows from this by a direct computation.

We conclude that

\displaystyle\bra{\psi_t}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\Big(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi)\Big)\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}

\displaystyle=(N-t+1)\bra{\psi_t^{\mathrm{pre}}}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist},t-1}^{Y}\Big(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi)\Big)\ket{\psi_t^{\mathrm{pre}}}

\displaystyle=(N-t+1)\bra{\psi_{t-1}}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist},t-1}^{Y}(A_{t}^{\dagger})_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}\Big(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi)\Big)(A_{t})_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist},t-1}^{Y}\ket{\psi_{t-1}}

\displaystyle=(N-t+1)\bra{\psi_{t-1}}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist},t-1}^{Y}\Big(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi)\Big)\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist},t-1}^{Y}\ket{\psi_{t-1}}.

This completes the induction.

Finally, using

\Omega_{S_{t}}=\frac{1}{t!}\sum_{\pi\in S_{t}}S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi),

the cross terms below vanish and so

\left\|\Omega_{S_{t}}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}\right\|^{2}=\frac{1}{t!}\left\|\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}\right\|^{2}=\binom{N}{t}.\qed

Lemma 6.5

(Reweighting is approximately scalar)
.

Assume
t^{2}=O(N)
. Then

\left\|\Lambda_{S_{t}}-\alpha_{t}\mathbf{I}\right\|\leq\alpha_{t}\cdot O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right).

Equivalently,

\displaystyle\alpha_{t}^{2}\left(1-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)\right)\mathbf{I}\preceq\Lambda_{S_{t}}^{\dagger}\Lambda_{S_{t}}\preceq\alpha_{t}^{2}\left(1+O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)\right)\mathbf{I}.

(6.15)

Proof.

We equivalently calculate

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{N^{t}}{t!}\Lambda_{S_{t}}^{\dagger}\Lambda_{S_{t}}-\mathbf{I}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\frac{N^{t}}{t!}\left(\sum_{\lambda\in\widehat{S}_{t}}\beta_{\lambda,t}^{2}\Pi_{\lambda,t}\right)-\mathbf{I}\right\rVert

(6.16)

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\sum_{\lambda\in\widehat{S}_{t}}\left(\frac{N^{t}}{t!}\beta_{\lambda,t}^{2}-1\right)\Pi_{\lambda,t}\right\rVert

(6.17)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\lambda\in\widehat{S}_{t}}\left\lvert\frac{N^{t}}{t!}\beta_{\lambda,t}^{2}-1\right\rvert

(6.18)

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)\,.

(
Lemma

3.32
)

∎

Lemma 6.6

(The exact unitary recording is almost distinct on
\mathsf{R}_{Y}
)
.

\left\|\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)}}}\right\|^{2}\geq 1-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right).

Proof.

We have that

\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)}}}=\Lambda_{S_{t}}\Omega_{S_{t}}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}.

By
Lemma

6.5
,

\Lambda_{S_{t}}^{\dagger}\Lambda_{S_{t}}\succeq\alpha_{t}^{2}\left(1-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)\right)\mathbf{I},

and by
Lemma

6.4
,

\left\|\Omega_{S_{t}}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}\right\|^{2}=\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}^{2}}.

Together, these prove the lemma.
∎

Theorem 6.7
.

For any
t
-query adversary with
t^{2}=O(N)
,

\left\lVert\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{MH}}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{MH}}}\right\rvert-\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}}}\right\rvert\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\right\rVert_{1}\leq O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right).

Proof.

We write

\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)}}}-\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{MH}}}=\left(\Lambda_{S_{t}}-\alpha_{t}\mathbf{I}\right)\Omega_{S_{t}}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}.

By
Lemmas

6.5
and
6.4
,

\left\|\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)}}}-\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{MH}}}\right\|\leq\alpha_{t}\cdot O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}}=O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{MH}}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{MH}}}\right\rvert-\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}}}\right\rvert\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\right\rVert_{1}

(6.19)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\cdot\left\|\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)}}}-\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{MH}}}\right\|

(6.20)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right).

(6.21)

∎

This completes the full comparison between the
[
MH25
]
path-recording oracle and our exact unitary path recording oracle.

6.2
The Unitary Haar Cipher

We now consider the case of the unitary Haar cipher, which consists of a collection of keyed unitaries
U_{1},\,\dots,\,U_{K}
, each sampled independently from the Haar measure on
U(N)
.
The group is therefore
G=U(N)^{K}
, and the representation is its block-diagonal embedding:

\displaystyle\rho(U_{1},\,\dots,\,U_{K})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in[K]}\outerproduct*{k}{k}\otimes U_{k}\,.

(6.22)

This is the idealization of a pseudorandom unitary, in which the unitary for each key is chosen independently from the Haar measure.

As the reader might rightfully guess, we can get a recording oracle for the unitary Haar cipher by simply maintaining
K
different recordings of each of the
K
underlying unitaries. And in fact, we can compress it to size that grows only with the number of queries by only explicitly recording the keys whose individual recording has a non-zero number of entries.
This is in fact the correct intuition, and for practical purposes, it might suffice to stop there.
But the beauty of our framework is that it eliminates the guesswork. We can in fact just blindly take the representation above and plug it into our framework to recover this outcome.

For this, we must first consider the commutant algebra of the tensor power of the representation in
Equation

6.22
. It turns out to be the Schur representation of the diagram algebra that comes out of the wreath product
\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}
of the cyclic group mod
K
and the symmetric group.

Definition 6.8
.

The
K
-colored permutation group
is the wreath product
\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}
. That is, it is the group whose group elements
(f,\pi)\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}
are parameterized by

•

a “function”
f\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t}
(we often write group elements of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t}
as functions
f\,:\,[t]\to\mathbb{Z}_{K}
, or as vectors
\vec{f}=(f(1),\,\dots,\,f(t))
), and

•

a permutation
\pi\in S_{t}
,

with a group operation that is given by entry-wise addition in
\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t}
, composing permutations in
S_{t}
, and permuting a function
f\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t}
by a permutation
\pi\in S_{t}
whenever they commute past each other.
Specifically,

\displaystyle\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}=\left\langle(f,\pi)\;\middle|\;f\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t},\,\pi\in S_{t},\,(f_{1},\pi_{1})\cdot(f_{2},\pi_{2})=\big(f_{1}+\pi_{1}(f_{2}),\,\pi_{1}\circ\pi_{2}\big)\right\rangle

(6.23)

This group is also often called

•

the
K
-phased permutation group, since the
\mathbb{Z}_{K}
can be viewed as phases that come from
K
’th roots of unity,

•

the monomial unitary group with modulus
K
, or

•

the complex reflection group
G(K,1,t)
.

When specializing to
K=2
, it is also known as the hyperoctahedral group.

Note that when it is clear from context we will often write the group elements
(f,e_{S_{t}})
and
(e_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t}},\pi)
more simply as
f
and
\pi
, respectively,
48
48

48

e_{S_{t}}
and
e_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t}}
are the identity elements of the respective groups.

so that we can write any group element
(f,\pi)
as a product
f\,\pi
, or equivalently, as
\pi\,f^{\prime}
, for
f^{\prime}=\pi^{-1}(f)
.

The defining representation of the colored permutation group is often taken to be as permutation matrices with nonzero entries taken from the
K
’th roots of unity.
We can also view group elements as colored permutation diagrams, where each strand has an attached “color” in
\mathbb{Z}_{K}
:

f_{1}
f_{2}
f_{3}
f_{4}
f_{5}
f_{6}

(6.24)

Figure 16
:
Colored permutation diagram on
6
elements with colors
f_{1},\dots,f_{6}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}
attached to the strands.

Diagram multiplication occurs by concatenation of the underlying permutations and summing (mod
K
) the colors along each merged strand.

We will be interested here in the Schur representation of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}
on
(\mathbb{C}^{KN})^{\otimes t}
, which generalizes the Schur representation of
S_{t}
(
Definition

3.16
) by adding a phase of
\omega_{K}^{f_{i}k_{i}}
whenever a register containing
\ket{k_i,\, x_i}
passes through a strand with color
f_{i}
.

For example,

S\!\left(\vbox{\hbox{

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
