{{}}{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@fill\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 137.8 -39.37 M 140.56 -39.37 C 140.56 -37.84 139.32 -36.6 137.8 -36.6 C 136.27 -36.6 135.03 -37.84 135.03 -39.37 C 135.03 -40.9 136.27 -42.14 137.8 -42.14 C 139.32 -42.14 140.56 -40.9 140.56 -39.37 Z M 137.8 -39.37}{stroke:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\par\par\par
\par\par\par{{{}}}{{{}}}{{{}}}{{{}}}\par\par\par\color[rgb]{0.57,0.3,0.03}\par{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 82.68 39.37 C 82.68 -15.75 165.35 15.75 165.35 -39.37}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{119.42831pt}{-20.00476pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \par{{}}{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@fill\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 82.68 39.37 M 85.44 39.37 C 85.44 40.9 84.21 42.14 82.68 42.14 C 81.15 42.14 79.91 40.9 79.91 39.37 C 79.91 37.84 81.15 36.6 82.68 36.6 C 84.21 36.6 85.44 37.84 85.44 39.37 Z M 82.68 39.37}{stroke:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{{}}{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@fill\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 165.35 -39.37 M 168.12 -39.37 C 168.12 -37.84 166.88 -36.6 165.35 -36.6 C 163.83 -36.6 162.59 -37.84 162.59 -39.37 C 162.59 -40.9 163.83 -42.14 165.35 -42.14 C 166.88 -42.14 168.12 -40.9 168.12 -39.37 Z M 165.35 -39.37}{stroke:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\par\par\par
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}
}}}\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{k}\in[K]^{6}\\
\vec{x}\in[N]^{6}\end{subarray}}\omega_{K}^{f_{1}k_{1}+\dots+f_{6}k_{6}}\;\begin{subarray}{c}\phantom{\big(}\\
\ket{
\vphantom{\big(}
(k_2, x_2),
(k_4, x_4),
(k_6, x_6),
(k_1, x_1),
(k_5, x_5),
(k_3, x_3)
}\\
\bra{
\vphantom{\big(}
(k_1, x_1),
(k_2, x_2),
(k_3, x_3),
(k_4, x_4),
(k_5, x_5),
(k_6, x_6)
}\end{subarray}\,.

(6.25)

Formally,

\displaystyle S\big(\pi\,f\big):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{k}\in[K]^{t}\\
\vec{x}\in[N]^{t}\end{subarray}}\;\omega_{K}^{\vec{f}\,\cdot\,\vec{k}}\outerproduct{
(k_{\pi^{-1}(1)}, x_{\pi^{-1}(1)}),
\dots,
(k_{\pi^{-1}(t)}, x_{\pi^{-1}(t)})
}{
(k_{\pi^{-1}(1)}, x_{\pi^{-1}(1)}),
\dots,
(k_{\pi^{-1}(t)}, x_{\pi^{-1}(t)})
}{(k_{1},x_{1}),\dots,(k_{t},x_{t})}

(6.26)

The
colored permutation algebra
is the group algebra
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}]
, which is the diagram algebra spanned by colored permutation diagrams.

Lemma 6.9
.

The commutant of
\rho(\vec{U})^{\otimes t}
(see
Equation

6.22
) is the Schur representation of the colored permutation algebra
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}]
.

Proof.

First, we show that
S(\pi\,f)
commutes with
\rho(\vec{U})^{\otimes t}
.

\displaystyle S(\pi\,f)\;\rho(\vec{U})^{\otimes t}

\displaystyle=S(\pi)\;S(f)\;\rho(\vec{U})^{\otimes t}

(6.27)

\displaystyle=S(\pi)\;\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{k}\in[K]^{t}\end{subarray}}\omega_{K}^{\vec{f}\,\cdot\,\vec{k}}\outerproduct*{
\vec k
}{
\vec k
}\otimes\mathbf{I}\right)\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{k}\in[K]^{t}\end{subarray}}\outerproduct*{\vec k}{\vec k}\otimes U_{k_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes U_{k_{t}}\right)

(6.28)

\displaystyle=S(\pi)\;\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{k}\in[K]^{t}\end{subarray}}\omega_{K}^{\vec{f}\,\cdot\,\vec{k}}\outerproduct*{\vec k}{\vec k}\otimes U_{k_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes U_{k_{t}}\right)

(6.29)

\displaystyle=S(\pi)\;\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{k}\in[K]^{t}\end{subarray}}\outerproduct*{\vec k}{\vec k}\otimes U_{k_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes U_{k_{t}}\right)\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{k}\in[K]^{t}\end{subarray}}\omega_{K}^{\vec{f}\,\cdot\,\vec{k}}\outerproduct*{
\vec k
}{
\vec k
}\otimes\mathbf{I}\right)

(6.30)

\displaystyle=S(\pi)\;\rho(\vec{U})^{\otimes t}\;S(f)

(6.31)

\displaystyle=\rho(\vec{U})^{\otimes t}\;S(\pi)\;S(f)

(6.32)

This shows that
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}]
is contained in the commutant.

It now remains to prove that every operator that commutes with
\rho(\vec{U})^{\otimes t}
must have the form
S(a)
for some
a\in\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}]
.
Let
T\in\mathrm{End}((\mathbb{C}^{KN})^{\otimes t})
be a generic operator on this space, and let’s say that it commutes with
\rho(\vec{U})^{\otimes t}
for all tuples of unitaries
\vec{U}\in U(N)^{K}
.
We first write
T
in terms of its blocks as

\displaystyle T=\sum_{\vec{p},\,\vec{q}\in[K]^{t}}|\vec{p}\rangle\!\langle\vec{q}|\otimes T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}

(6.33)

where the

|\vec{p}\rangle\!\langle\vec{q}|

acts on all the control (or key) registers,
and each

T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}\in\mathrm{End}((\mathbb{C}^{N})^{\otimes t})

acts on the rest of the registers (where each of the unitaries
U_{k_{i}}
acts).

We know that for all
\vec{U}
, we have

[\rho(\vec{U})^{\otimes t},\,T]=\rho(\vec{U})^{\otimes t}\,T-T\,\rho(\vec{U})^{\otimes t}=0
.
For brevity, we write

U_{\vec{k}}\coloneqq U_{k_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes U_{k_{t}}
.
The commutator is then

\displaystyle\rho(\vec{U})^{\otimes t}\,T-T\,\rho(\vec{U})^{\otimes t}

(6.34)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{k}\in[K]^{t}\end{subarray}}\outerproduct*{\vec k}{\vec k}\otimes U_{\vec{k}}\right)\,\left(\sum_{\vec{p},\,\vec{q}\in[K]^{t}}|\vec{p}\rangle\!\langle\vec{q}|\otimes T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}\right)-\left(\sum_{\vec{p},\,\vec{q}\in[K]^{t}}|\vec{p}\rangle\!\langle\vec{q}|\otimes T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}\right)\,\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{k}\in[K]^{t}\end{subarray}}\outerproduct*{\vec k}{\vec k}\otimes U_{\vec{k}}\right)

(6.35)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{k},\,\vec{p},\,\vec{q}\in[K]^{t}\end{subarray}}\outerproduct*{\vec k}{\vec k}\cdot|\vec{p}\rangle\!\langle\vec{q}|\otimes U_{\vec{k}}\cdot T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}\right)-\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{k},\,\vec{p},\,\vec{q}\in[K]^{t}\end{subarray}}|\vec{p}\rangle\!\langle\vec{q}|\cdot\outerproduct*{\vec k}{\vec k}\otimes T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}\cdot U_{\vec{k}}\right)

(6.36)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{p},\,\vec{q}\in[K]^{t}\end{subarray}}|\vec{p}\rangle\!\langle\vec{q}|\otimes U_{\vec{p}}\cdot T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}\right)-\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{p},\,\vec{q}\in[K]^{t}\end{subarray}}|\vec{p}\rangle\!\langle\vec{q}|\otimes T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}\cdot U_{\vec{q}}\right)

(6.37)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{p},\,\vec{q}\in[K]^{t}\end{subarray}}|\vec{p}\rangle\!\langle\vec{q}|\otimes\left(U_{\vec{p}}\cdot T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}-T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}\cdot U_{\vec{q}}\right)\,.

(6.38)

Now, if this operator is
0
, then it must be
0
on each block. That is, for every
\vec{p},\vec{q}\in[K]^{t}
(and every
\vec{U}
), we have that

U_{\vec{p}}\cdot T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}-T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}\cdot U_{\vec{q}}=0
.

Since this holds for all
\vec{U}\in U(N)^{K}
, it must also hold for the case where each unitary is simply
U_{k}=e^{i\,\theta_{k}}\,\mathbf{I}
. In this case, we have that

\displaystyle U_{\vec{p}}\cdot T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}-T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}\cdot U_{\vec{q}}

\displaystyle=e^{i\sum_{j\in[t]}\theta_{p_{j}}}T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}-e^{i\sum_{j\in[t]}\theta_{q_{j}}}T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}

(6.39)

\displaystyle=\left(e^{i\sum_{j\in[t]}\theta_{p_{j}}}-e^{i\sum_{j\in[t]}\theta_{q_{j}}}\right)T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}\,.

(6.40)

Therefore,
T_{\vec{p},\,\vec{q}}
must be
0
unless
\vec{p}
is a permutation of
\vec{q}
(that is, they have the same entries in a different order).

It must also hold when all the unitaries are identical (that is
U_{k}=U
for all
k
). In this case, we have

U_{\vec{p}}\cdot T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}-T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}\cdot U_{\vec{q}}=U^{\otimes t}\cdot T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}-T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}\cdot U^{\otimes t}
,
and thus from classical Schur-Weyl duality, we know that the blocks of
T
must be in the span of the permutations:

T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}=\sum_{\pi\in S_{t}}\,c^{\vec{p},\vec{q}}_{\pi}\,S(\pi)
,
for some coefficients
c^{\vec{p},\vec{q}}_{\pi}
.

Now for the full group
U(N)^{K}
, we have that

\displaystyle U_{\vec{p}}\cdot T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}-T_{\vec{p},\vec{q}}\cdot U_{\vec{q}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in S_{t}\end{subarray}}c^{\vec{p},\vec{q}}_{\pi}\left(U_{\vec{p}}\cdot S(\pi)-S(\pi)\cdot U_{\vec{q}}\right)

(6.41)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in S_{t}\end{subarray}}c^{\vec{p},\vec{q}}_{\pi}\left(S(\pi)\cdot U_{\pi^{-1}(\vec{p})}-S(\pi)\cdot U_{\vec{q}}\right)

(6.42)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in S_{t}\end{subarray}}c^{\vec{p},\vec{q}}_{\pi}\,S(\pi)\left(U_{\pi^{-1}(\vec{p})}-U_{\vec{q}}\right)\,.

(6.43)

since the
S(\pi)
operators are linearly independent, it must hold that for all
\pi\in S_{t}
,

c^{\vec{p},\vec{q}}_{\pi}\,\left(U_{\pi^{-1}(\vec{p})}-U_{\vec{q}}\right)=0
.
So
c^{\vec{p},\vec{q}}_{\pi}=0
unless
\vec{p}=\pi(\vec{q})
.

We thus have that

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{q}\in[K]^{t}\\
\pi\in S_{t}\end{subarray}}c^{\pi(\vec{q}),\vec{q}}_{\pi}\,|\pi(\vec{q})\rangle\!\langle\vec{q}|\otimes S(\pi)

(6.44)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{q}\in[K]^{t}\\
\pi\in S_{t}\end{subarray}}c^{\pi(\vec{q}),\vec{q}}_{\pi}\,S(\pi)\left(|\vec{q}\rangle\!\langle\vec{q}|\otimes\mathbf{I}\right)\,,

(6.45)

where in the first line,
S(\pi)
acts on only the second register, while in the second line
S(\pi)
acts on both registers together.
Thus,
T
is in the span of operators of the form

M_{\pi,\vec{q}}\coloneqq S(\pi)\left(|\vec{q}\rangle\!\langle\vec{q}|\otimes\mathbf{I}\right)
.
It thus remains to show that
M_{\pi,\vec{q}}
is in the span of
S(\pi f)
for
(\pi,f)\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}
. To this end, observe that

\displaystyle|\vec{q}\rangle\!\langle\vec{q}|\otimes\mathbf{I}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{K^{t}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{f}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t}\end{subarray}}\!\omega_{K}^{-\vec{f}\cdot\vec{q}}\;\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{r}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t}\end{subarray}}\omega_{K}^{\vec{f}\cdot\vec{r}}\;\outerproduct*{
\vec r
}{
\vec r
}\otimes\mathbf{I}\right)

(6.46)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{K^{t}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{f}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t}\end{subarray}}\!\omega_{K}^{-\vec{f}\cdot\vec{q}}\;S(f)\,.

(6.47)

Thus
T
is in the span of
S(\pi f)
, which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.10
.

The irreps of both the group
G=U(N)^{K}
and the commutant algebra
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}]
are parameterized by
K
-tuples of Young diagrams,

\left(\lambda^{(1)},\,\lambda^{(2)},\,\dots,\,\lambda^{(K)}\right)
,
where each
\lambda^{(i)}\in\widehat{S}_{t_{i}}\cap\widehat{U(N)}
, and
\sum_{i\in[K]}t_{i}=t
.

Furthermore, the dimensions of the irreps are as follows:

Since the group
U(N)^{K}
is a direct product, its irreps are also direct products of irreps of
U(N)
, and so the dimension of an irrep

\vec{\lambda}\coloneqq\left(\lambda^{(1)},\,\lambda^{(2)},\,\dots,\,\lambda^{(K)}\right)

is the product of the individual dimensions

\displaystyle\dim\left(V_{U(N)^{K}}^{\vec{\lambda}}\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i\in[K]}\dim\left(V_{U(N)}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\right)\,.

(6.48)

Similarly, the dimension of the corresponding irrep of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}
is
[
Str24
,
Mac98
]

\displaystyle\dim\left(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}}^{\vec{\lambda}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{t!}{\prod_{i\in[K]}(t_{i}!)}\prod_{i\in[K]}\dim\left(V_{S_{t_{i}}}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\right)=\frac{t!}{\prod_{i\in[K]}(t_{i}!)}\prod_{i\in[K]}f^{\lambda^{(i)}}\,.

(6.49)

Therefore, the irrep ratios are

\displaystyle\frac{\dim\left(V_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}}^{\vec{\lambda}}\right)}{\dim\left(V_{U(N)^{K}}^{\vec{\lambda}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{t!}{\prod_{i\in[K]}(t_{i}!)}\prod_{i\in[K]}\frac{\dim\left(V_{S_{t_{i}}}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\right)}{\dim\left(V_{U(N)}^{\lambda^{(i)}}\right)}

(6.50)

\displaystyle=\frac{t!}{\prod_{i\in[K]}(t_{i}!)}\prod_{i\in[K]}\frac{t_{i}!}{N^{t_{i}}}\left(1\pm O\left(\frac{t_{i}^{2}}{N}\right)\right)

(
Lemma

3.32
)

\displaystyle=\frac{t!}{N^{t}}\left(1\pm O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)\right)

(
\sum_{i}t_{i}^{2}\leq\left(\sum_{i}t_{i}\right)^{2}=t^{2}
)

Note that since the colored permutation algebra is a group algebra, its dual basis (
Example

3.18
) is proportional to the inverse of each group element:

\displaystyle[\pi\,f]^{*}=\frac{1}{t!\,K^{t}}[f^{-1}\pi^{-1}]\,.

(6.51)

6.2.1
Recording Oracle for the Unitary Haar Cipher

We will use the following notation for the registers:
Let the adversary’s query register be split as
\mathsf{A}\coloneqq(\mathsf{A_{k}},\mathsf{A_{u}})
, corresponding respectively to the key/control register, and the register on which the unitary
U_{k}
acts. That is, the query representation acts as
\rho(\vec{U})=\sum_{k\in[K]}\outerproduct*{k}{k}_{\mathsf{A_{k}}}\otimes\left(U_{k}\right)_{\mathsf{A_{u}}}
.

Similarly, let the purification register
\mathsf{R}
of the recording be split into
t
registers
\mathsf{R}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{R}_{t}
, and let each
\mathsf{R}_{i}
contain a 4-tuple
(\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{k}},i},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{u}},i},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{Y_{k}},i},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{Y_{u}},i})
, with
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X},i}\coloneqq(\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{k}},i},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{u}},i})
, and
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{Y},i}\coloneqq(\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{Y_{k}},i},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{Y_{u}},i})
. Let

\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{k}}}:=(\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{k}},1},\dots,\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{k}},t})

\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{u}}}:=(\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{u}},1},\dots,\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{u}},t})
, and so forth.

We have from
Equation

5.40

that the path recording update rule for the unitary Haar cipher,
G=U(N)^{K}
, has the form

\displaystyle V_{U(N)^{K}}:=\Big(\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\;\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}\Big(\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{i-1}}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i-1}}\;,

(6.52)

and from
Corollary

5.18
that a
t
-query adaptive algorithm
\mathsf{Adv}_{t}
querying
V_{U(N)^{K}}
has the form
49
49

49

when starting with an empty recording. Otherwise, we of course also have an additional rescaling of

\left(\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{|\mathsf{R_{0}}|}}^{+}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{0}}

at the beginning.

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)^{K}}}

\displaystyle=\Big(\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}}\cdot\Big(\Omega_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\,.

(6.53)

To understand the path recording oracle for the unitary Haar cipher, we therefore only need to look at the form of
\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}}
and

\Omega_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}}
.

Lemma 6.11

(Factoring
\Omega
along the wreath product)
.

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}}=\Omega_{S_{t}}\,\Omega_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t}}

(6.54)

Proof.

We have that

\displaystyle S\big(\pi\,f\big)

\displaystyle=S\big(\pi\big)\,S\big(f\big)\,,

(6.55)

and

\displaystyle S\big((\pi\,f)^{*}\big)^{T}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t!\,K^{t}}S\left(f^{-1}\pi^{-1}\right)^{T}

(by
Equation

6.51
)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t!\,K^{t}}\left(S\left(\pi^{-1}\right)\,S\left(f^{-1}\right)\right)^{T}

(6.56)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t!\,K^{t}}S\left(\pi^{-1}\right)^{T}\,S\left(f^{-1}\right)^{T}

(6.57)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t!\,K^{t}}S\left(\pi\right)\,\overline{S\left(f\right)}\,,

(since
S(\pi)
is real, and
S(f)
is diagonal)

and therefore

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\pi f\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}}S(\pi f)\otimes S((\pi f)^{*})^{T}

(
Lemma

5.11
)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t!\,K^{t}}\sum_{\pi f\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}}S\big(\pi\big)\,S\big(f\big)\otimes S\left(\pi\right)\,\overline{S\left(f\right)}

(6.58)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{t!}\sum_{\pi\in S_{t}}S\big(\pi\big)\otimes S\left(\pi\right)\right)\left(\frac{1}{K^{t}}\sum_{f\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t}}S\big(f\big)\otimes\overline{S\left(f\right)}\right)

(6.59)

\displaystyle=\Omega_{S_{t}}\,\Omega_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t}}\qed

(6.60)

Note that the same argument also shows that

\Omega_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}}=\Omega_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t}}\,\Omega_{S_{t}}
(just write the elements of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}
as
f^{\prime}\pi
instead of
\pi f
),
implying that
\Omega_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t}}
and
\Omega_{S_{t}}
commute.

Lemma 6.12
.

\displaystyle\left(\Omega_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{k}}},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{u}}},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{Y_{k}}},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{Y_{u}}}}

\displaystyle=\;\;\Big(\Pi_{\mathsf{X_{k}=Y_{k}}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{k}}},\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{Y_{k}}}}

(6.61)

where

\Pi_{\mathsf{X_{k}=Y_{k}}}

is the projector onto the registers
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{X_{k}}}
and
\mathsf{R}_{\mathsf{Y_{k}}}
being equal in the standard basis.

Proof.

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{K^{t}}\sum_{f\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t}}S\big(f\big)\otimes\overline{S\left(f\right)}

(6.62)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{K^{t}}\sum_{f\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{k}\in[K]^{t}\\
\vec{x}\in[N]^{t}\end{subarray}}\omega_{K}^{\vec{f}\,\cdot\,\vec{k}}\outerproduct{
\vec k,
\vec x
}{
\vec k,
\vec x
}{\vec{k},\vec{x}}_{\mathsf{R_{X_{k}}},\mathsf{R_{X_{u}}}}\otimes\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{k^{\prime}}\in[K]^{t}\\
\vec{y}\in[N]^{t}\end{subarray}}\omega_{K}^{-\vec{f}\,\cdot\,\vec{k}^{\prime}}\outerproduct{
\vec k',
\vec y
}{
\vec k',
\vec y
}{\vec{k}^{\prime},\vec{y}}_{\mathsf{R_{Y_{k}}},\mathsf{R_{Y_{u}}}}

(6.63)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{k},\vec{k^{\prime}}\in[K]^{t}\\
\vec{x},\vec{y}\in[N]^{t}\end{subarray}}\underbrace{\left(\frac{1}{K^{t}}\sum_{f\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t}}\omega_{K}^{\vec{f}\,\cdot\,(\vec{k}-\vec{k}^{\prime})}\right)}_{\delta_{\vec{k},\vec{k}^{\prime}}}\outerproduct{
\vec k,
\vec x
}{
\vec k,
\vec x
}{\vec{k},\vec{x}}_{\mathsf{R_{X_{k}}},\mathsf{R_{X_{u}}}}\otimes\outerproduct{
\vec k',
\vec y
}{
\vec k',
\vec y
}{\vec{k}^{\prime},\vec{y}}_{\mathsf{R_{Y_{k}}},\mathsf{R_{Y_{u}}}}

(6.64)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{k}\in[K]^{t}\\
\vec{x},\vec{y}\in[N]^{t}\end{subarray}}\outerproduct{
\vec k,
\vec x
}{
\vec k,
\vec x
}{\vec{k},\vec{x}}_{\mathsf{R_{X_{k}}},\mathsf{R_{X_{u}}}}\otimes\outerproduct{
\vec k,
\vec y
}{
\vec k,
\vec y
}{\vec{k},\vec{y}}_{\mathsf{R_{Y_{k}}},\mathsf{R_{Y_{u}}}}

(6.65)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{k}\in[K]^{t}\end{subarray}}\outerproduct{
\vec k,
\vec k,
}{
\vec k,
\vec k,
}{\vec{k},\vec{k},}_{\mathsf{R_{X_{k}}},\mathsf{R_{Y_{k}}}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{\mathsf{R_{X_{u}}},\mathsf{R_{Y_{u}}}}\qed

(6.66)

We see, therefore, that
\Omega_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}^{t}}
plays the role of ensuring that the control register remains the same before and after the query, and moreover, that the same key is recorded in both the
\mathsf{R_{X}}
and the
\mathsf{R_{Y}}
halves of the recording.
That is, the recording will always have pairs of the form
(k_{i},x_{i},k_{i},y_{i})
.
Specifically, by the following lemma, we have that when the
\mathsf{R}
register is already correctly formatted in a state of this form,
the update rule without the rescaling proceeds by appending a new tuple of the form
(k_{i},x_{i},k_{i},y_{i})
, and symmetrizing over all permutations with the existing tuples.

Lemma 6.13
.

\displaystyle\Big(\Omega_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\;\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}=\Big(\Omega_{S_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\;\Big(\operatorname{App}_{\left(\mathsf{X_{k}}=\mathsf{Y_{k}}\right)}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}\Big(\Pi_{\mathsf{X_{k}=Y_{k}}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq{i-1}}}

(6.67)

where

\displaystyle\operatorname{App}_{\left(\mathsf{X_{k}}=\mathsf{Y_{k}}\right)}\coloneqq\sum_{k\in[K],\,x,y\in[N]}\outerproduct{k, y}{k, x}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\ket{k, x, k, y}_{\mathsf{R}_{i}}

(6.68)

Proof.

\displaystyle\Big(\Omega_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}

\displaystyle=\Big(\Omega_{S_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Pi_{\mathsf{X_{k}=Y_{k}}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}

(6.69)

\displaystyle=\Big(\Omega_{S_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Pi_{\mathsf{X_{k}=Y_{k}}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{i}}\Big(\Pi_{\mathsf{X_{k}=Y_{k}}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i-1}}\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}

(6.70)

\displaystyle=\Big(\Omega_{S_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Pi_{\mathsf{X_{k}=Y_{k}}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{i}}\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}\Big(\Pi_{\mathsf{X_{k}=Y_{k}}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i-1}}

(6.71)

\displaystyle=\Big(\Omega_{S_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k,k^{\prime}\in[K]\\
x,y\in[N]\end{subarray}}\outerproduct{k', y}{k, x}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\Pi_{\mathsf{X_{k}=Y_{k}}}\ket{k, x, k', y}_{\mathsf{R}_{i}}\right)\Big(\Pi_{\mathsf{X_{k}=Y_{k}}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i-1}}

(6.72)

\displaystyle=\Big(\Omega_{S_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[K]\\
x,y\in[N]\end{subarray}}\outerproduct{k, y}{k, x}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\ket{k, x, k, y}_{\mathsf{R}_{i}}\right)\Big(\Pi_{\mathsf{X_{k}=Y_{k}}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i-1}}

(6.73)

\displaystyle=\Big(\Omega_{S_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\operatorname{App}_{\left(\mathsf{X_{k}}=\mathsf{Y_{k}}\right)}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}\Big(\Pi_{\mathsf{X_{k}=Y_{k}}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq{i-1}}}

∎

Corollary 6.14
.

Assuming we start with a validly formatted recording register, we can write the path recording update rule for the unitary Haar cipher as

\displaystyle V_{U(N)^{K}}=\Big(\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{S_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\;\Big(\operatorname{App}_{\left(\mathsf{X_{k}}=\mathsf{Y_{k}}\right)}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}\Big(\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{i-1}}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i-1}}\;,

(6.74)

and we can write a
t
-query adaptive algorithm
\mathsf{Adv}_{t}
querying
V_{U(N)^{K}}
starting with an empty recording as

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)^{K}}}

\displaystyle=\Big(\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}}\cdot\Big(\Omega_{S_{t}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}_{\left(\mathsf{X_{k}}=\mathsf{Y_{k}}\right)}}\,.

(6.75)

Thus, we have that up to the reweighting operator
\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}}
(which for the sake of simplicity, we can safely set aside, as it is
O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)
-close to a scalar multiple of the identity, see
Lemma

6.10
50
50

50

A more careful treatment can show that even the
\Lambda_{\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}}
operator can be properly decomposed, and it is therefore not necessary to incur any error in transforming this path recording to a
K
-tuple of individual path recordings. Since this is slightly more involved and offers little additional clarity or intuition at this point, we will not do so here. However, we do work this out explicitly in the special case of diagonal unitaries (
N=1
) below.

), our recording oracle is encoding multiset states of the form

\displaystyle\ket{
\left\{
(k_1, x_1, y_1),
(k_2, x_2, y_2),
\,\dots,
(k_t, x_t, y_t)
\right\}
}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\Omega_{S_{t}}\ket{
(k_1, x_1, k_1, y_1),
(k_2, x_2, k_2, y_2),
\,\dots,
(k_t, x_t, k_t, y_t)
}\,,

(6.76)

and each
i
’th query adds
(k_{i},x_{i},y_{i})
to the set (by appending
(\operatorname{App})
and then reshuffling the order
(\Omega_{S_{t}})
).
We can, of course, alternatively associate each such multiset state with a
K
-tuple
(R_{1},\dots,R_{K})
of multisets of the form
R_{k}=\{(x_{i},y_{i})\;|\;k_{i}=k\}
, where the
k
’th multiset in the
K
-tuple contains the
(x,y)
pairs corresponding to querying the
k
’th unitary.

6.2.2
Diagonal Unitaries

Specializing further, we can take
N=1
, to get the group
U(1)^{K}
of
K
-dimensional diagonal unitaries (the
K
-torus). In this case, each
U_{k}
in
Equation

6.22
is simply a unit-norm complex phase, and
x_{i}
and
y_{i}
are 0-qubit registers, and therefore disappear from the update rule, dropping out of
Equation

6.76
to give recording states of the form

\displaystyle\ket{
\left\{
k_1,
k_2,
\,\dots,
k_t
\right\}
}

\displaystyle:\propto\Omega_{S_{t}}\ket{
(k_1, k_1),
(k_2, k_2),
\,\dots,
(k_t, k_t)
}\,.

(6.77)

Furthermore, the commutant of
\rho(\vec{U})^{\otimes t}
is still a representation of
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}]
, but it is no longer a faithful representation: as noted in
Lemma

6.10
since
t>N=1
, the irreps
\vec{\lambda}
that survive are those where each
\lambda^{(i)}
indexes an irrep of
U(1)
. That is, we have that each
\lambda^{(i)}
is a Young diagram with a single row. Let
\mathcal{A}_{t}
be this quotient algebra, that is
\mathcal{A}_{t}\coloneqq\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}]/\ker(S)
(where
S(\cdot)
is the Schur representation of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}
, see
Equation

6.26
).

Lemma 6.15
.

Let
G=U(1)^{K}
be the group of
K
dimensional diagonal unitaries, with representation
\rho(\theta_{1},\dots,\theta_{K})=\sum_{k\in[K]}e^{-i\theta_{k}}\outerproduct*{k}{k}
. Then the path recording update rule for this group simplifies to a multiset insertion

\displaystyle V_{U(1)^{K}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[K]\\
R\textnormal{ multiset from }[K]\end{subarray}}\outerproduct*{k}{k}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\outerproduct{R \uplus\{k\}}{R\,}_{\mathsf{R}}

(6.78)

on multiset states of the form

\ket{
\left\{
k_1,
k_2,
\dots,
k_t
\right\}
}\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{t!\prod_{i\in[K]}(t_{i}!)}}\sum_{\pi\in S_{t}}\ket{
\pi(
k_1,
k_2,
\dots,
k_t
)
}
,
where
t_{i}
is the number of times
i
appears in the multiset.
51
51

51

Note that when the multiset contains collisions/repeats, different permutations can give the same basis string, and so the prefactor provides the proper normalization to make this a normalized state.

Proof.

We start with the recording oracle for
U(N)^{K}
from
Corollary

6.14
, specialized to the case where
N=1
:

\displaystyle V_{U(1)^{K}}=\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{S_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\;\Big(\operatorname{App}_{\left(\mathsf{X_{k}}=\mathsf{Y_{k}}\right)}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i-1}}\;,

(6.79)

Note that the purification register here contains both an
\mathsf{R_{X}}=(\mathsf{R_{X}}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{R_{X}}_{t})
and a corresponding
\mathsf{R_{Y}}=(\mathsf{R_{Y}}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{R_{Y}}_{t})
. But we know by
Corollary

6.14
that
\mathsf{R_{X}}
and
\mathsf{R_{Y}}
will contain identical strings (recall that we have thrown out the part of
\mathsf{R}
that was not identical when we took
N=1
).
So of course, there is redundancy.
We can therefore write the update rule here as if it were acting on just a single purification register
\mathsf{R}=(\mathsf{R}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{R}_{t})
containing a string of
t

k
-values,
These are off by a simple relabeling on the purification register, so this distinction will not matter, but it will simplify notation, so we will write it here as if it were a single register
\mathsf{R}=(\mathsf{R}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{R}_{t})
.
We thus replace

\Big(\operatorname{App}_{\left(\mathsf{X_{k}}=\mathsf{Y_{k}}\right)}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}=\sum_{k\in[K]}\outerproduct*{k}{k}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\ket{k, k}_{\mathsf{R_{X}}_{i}\,\mathsf{R_{Y}}_{i}}

with

\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}=\sum_{k\in[K]}\outerproduct*{k}{k}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\ket{k}_{\mathsf{R}_{i}}

and write

\Omega_{S_{t}}

as

\frac{1}{t!}\sum_{\pi\in S_{t}}S(\pi)
.

Now, we argue about the reweighting operators
\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}
and
\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}^{+}
:
Consider the irrep ratios in
Lemma

6.10
. The group
U(1)
is Abelian, which implies that all the irreps in the denominator of
Equation

6.50
have dimension 1, and furthermore, the irreps of
U(1)
are parameterized by Young diagrams of a single-row, which means that each
\lambda^{(i)}
in the numerator is the trivial irrep of the corresponding symmetric group
S_{t_{i}}
, all of which have dimension 1.
We thus have that the irrep ratios simplify to

\frac{t!}{\prod_{i\in[K]}(t_{i}!)}
, where
t_{i}
is the size of
\lambda^{(i)}
, or the number of times that
i
appears in the recording register.
Therefore, the reweighting operator (for this case when
N=1
) produces a type-dependent scalar:

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(t_{1},\dots,t_{K})\end{subarray}}\sqrt{\frac{t!}{\prod_{i\in[K]}(t_{i}!)}}\;\Pi_{(t_{1},\dots,t_{K})}\,,\;\;\text{ and }\;\;\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{t-1}}^{+}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(t_{1},\dots,t_{K})\end{subarray}}\sqrt{\frac{\prod_{i\in[K]}(t_{i}!)}{t!}}\;\Pi_{(t_{1},\dots,t_{K})}\,,

(6.80)

where

\Pi_{(t_{1},\dots,t_{K})}

is the projector onto strings with a type of the form
(t_{1},\dots,t_{K})
.

We thus have that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}\Omega_{S_{t}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(t_{1},\dots,t_{K})\end{subarray}}\sqrt{\frac{t!}{\prod_{i\in[K]}(t_{i}!)}}\;\Pi_{(t_{1},\dots,t_{K})}\cdot\frac{1}{t!}\sum_{\pi\in S_{t}}S(\pi)

(6.81)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(t_{1},\dots,t_{K})\end{subarray}}\sqrt{\frac{t!}{\prod_{i\in[K]}(t_{i}!)}}\;\cdot\frac{1}{t!}\sum_{\pi\in S_{t}}S(\pi)\;\Pi_{(t_{1},\dots,t_{K})}

(
\Pi_{(t_{1},\dots,t_{K})}

is invariant under permutations
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(t_{1},\dots,t_{K})\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{t!\prod_{i\in[K]}(t_{i}!)}}\;\sum_{\pi\in S_{t}}S(\pi)\;\Pi_{(t_{1},\dots,t_{K})}

(6.82)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1},k_{2},\dots,k_{t}\in[K]\end{subarray}}\outerproduct{
\left\{
k_1,
k_2,
\dots,
k_t
\right\}
}{
\left\{
k_1,
k_2,
\dots,
k_t
\right\}
}{k_{1},k_{2},\dots,k_{t}}

(6.83)

\displaystyle=:\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{k}\in[K]^{t}\end{subarray}}\lvert\{\vec{k}\}\rangle\langle\vec{k}\rvert\,.

(6.84)

So this is the normalized symmetrization operator.
Similarly, we have that

\displaystyle\Omega_{S_{t-1}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{t-1}}^{+}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(t-1)!}\sum_{\pi\in S_{t-1}}S(\pi)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(t_{1},\dots,t_{K})\end{subarray}}\sqrt{\frac{\prod_{i\in[K]}(t_{i}!)}{(t-1)!}}\;\Pi_{(t_{1},\dots,t_{K})}

(6.85)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(t_{1},\dots,t_{K})\end{subarray}}\sqrt{\frac{\prod_{i\in[K]}(t_{i}!)}{(t-1)!}}\;\cdot\frac{1}{(t-1)!}\sum_{\pi\in S_{t-1}}\Pi_{(t_{1},\dots,t_{K})}\;S(\pi)

(
\Pi_{(t_{1},\dots,t_{K})}

is invariant under permutations
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(t_{1},\dots,t_{K})\end{subarray}}\frac{\prod_{i\in[K]}(t_{i}!)}{(t-1)!}\sqrt{\frac{1}{(t-1)!\prod_{i\in[K]}(t_{i}!)}}\sum_{\pi\in S_{t-1}}\Pi_{(t_{1},\dots,t_{K})}\;S(\pi)

(6.86)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1},k_{2},\dots,k_{t}\in[K]\end{subarray}}\frac{\prod_{i\in[K]}\left(t_{i}^{(\vec{k})}!\right)}{(t-1)!}\outerproduct{
k_1,
k_2,
\dots,
k_t
}{
k_1,
k_2,
\dots,
k_t
}{\left\{k_{1},k_{2},\dots,k_{t}\right\}}

(6.87)

\displaystyle=:\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{k}\in[K]^{t}\end{subarray}}\frac{\prod_{i\in[K]}\left(t_{i}^{(\vec{k})}!\right)}{(t-1)!}\lvert\vec{k}\rangle\langle\{\vec{k}\}\rvert\,.

(6.88)

where we take
t_{i}^{(\vec{k})}!
to be the number of times that a value
i\in[K]
appears inside
\vec{k}
.

We now combine everything to get the result:

\displaystyle V_{U(1)^{K}}

\displaystyle=\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{S_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i-1}}

(6.89)

\displaystyle=\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{S_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{S_{i-1}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq{i-1}}}\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i-1}}

(by
Lemma

5.17
)

\displaystyle=\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{S_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}\Big(\Omega_{S_{i-1}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq{i-1}}}\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i-1}}

(
\Omega_{S_{i-1}}
and
\operatorname{App}
act on different registers)

\displaystyle=\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{S_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\left(\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[K]\\
\phantom{}{}^{\phantom{i-1}}\vec{k}\in[K]^{i-1}\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\outerproduct*{k}{k}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\lvert\vec{k},k\rangle_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\langle\vec{k}\,\rvert_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq{i-1}}}\right)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\Big(\Omega_{S_{i-1}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq{i-1}}}\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i-1}}

(definition of
\operatorname{App}
)

\displaystyle=\left(\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\phantom{}{}^{\phantom{i-1}}\vec{k}^{\prime}\in[K]^{i-1}\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\lvert\{\vec{k}^{\prime}\}\rangle\langle\vec{k}^{\prime}\rvert\right)_{\!\!\!\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\left(\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[K]\\
\phantom{}{}^{\phantom{i-1}}\vec{k}\in[K]^{i-1}\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\outerproduct*{k}{k}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\lvert\vec{k},k\rangle_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\langle\vec{k}\,\rvert_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq{i-1}}}\right)_{\!\!\!\!\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}\!\!\!\!\left(\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\phantom{}{}^{\phantom{i-1}}\vec{k}^{\prime\prime}\in[K]^{i-1}\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\frac{\prod_{i\in[K]}\left(t_{i}^{(\vec{k}^{\prime\prime})}!\right)}{(t-1)!}\lvert\vec{k}^{\prime\prime}\rangle\langle\{\vec{k}^{\prime\prime}\}\rvert\right)_{\!\!\!\mathsf{R}_{\leq i-1}}

(by
Equations

6.84
and
6.88
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[K]\\
\phantom{}{}^{\phantom{i-1}}\vec{k}\in[K]^{i-1}\end{subarray}}\frac{\prod_{i\in[K]}\left(t_{i}^{(\vec{k})}!\right)}{(t-1)!}\outerproduct*{k}{k}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\lvert\{\vec{k}\}\uplus\{k\}\rangle_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\langle\{\vec{k}\}\rvert_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq{i-1}}}

(6.90)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in[K]\\
R\textnormal{ multiset from }[K]\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\outerproduct*{k}{k}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\ket{
R
\uplus\{
k
\}
}_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\bra{
R\,
}_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq{i-1}}}

(6.91)

Where the last equality follows since each multiset
R=\{\vec{k}\}
corresponds to

\frac{(t-1)!}{\prod_{i\in[K]}\left(t_{i}^{(\vec{k})}!\right)}

distinct strings. This completes the proof.
∎

We can see, therefore, that the path recording oracle for
U(1)^{K}
is simply keeping a running total that keeps track of how many times each key
k
has been queried.
This is, of course, the recording oracle of Zhandry
[
Zha19
]
for the case of queries to a Haar random diagonal unitary (that is, a function
f:[K]\to U(1)
).

6.3
Zhandry’s Compressed Phase Oracle

We can, of course, specialize this further to random Boolean functions,
f:\{0,1\}^{n}\to\{0,1\}
. Without loss of generality, we can view these functions as being applied in the phase:

\displaystyle\rho(f)=\sum_{x\in\{0,1\}^{n}}(-1)^{f(x)}\outerproduct*{x}{x}\,.

(6.92)

The group of such Boolean functions is thus
G=\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{N}
(again, taking
N=2^{n}
), with the group operation given by an XOR on the truth tables, and a representation as diagonal unitaries with
\pm 1
values.

Lemma 6.16
.

Let
G=\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{N}
be the group of Boolean functions, with the representation in
Equation

6.92
above. Then the path recording update rule for this group simplifies to a set insertion mod 2 (that is, add to the set if it does not yet appear, and remove it if it does):

\displaystyle V_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{N}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in[N]\\
R\subseteq[N]\end{subarray}}\outerproduct*{x}{x}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\outerproduct{R \oplus\{x\}}{R\,}_{\mathsf{R}}\,.

(6.93)

This is the compressed oracle of
[
Zha19
]
for Boolean functions applied in the phase.

We remark that Zhandry’s compressed oracle can also be derived from the tableau-recording oracle: since all irreducible representations of a finite Abelian group are one-dimensional (i.e., characters of the group), the relevant Clebsch-Gordan transforms all simplify to scalars. Nevertheless, we prove
Lemma

6.16
to demonstrate how the path-recording oracle can be used to derive a wide variety of existing compressed oracles, and to improve our general-purpose understanding of the path-recording oracle.

Proof.

We can easily compute the commutant of
\rho(f)^{\otimes t}
as follows.
Let
\vec{f}
be the bit string encoding of
f
as
(f(1),f(2),\dots,f(N))\in\{0,1\}^{N}
, and similarly let
m_{2}(x)\in\{0,1\}^{N}
be the one-hot encoding of
x\in[N]
. Then
f(x)=\vec{f}\cdot m_{2}(x)
.
We write

\displaystyle\rho(f)^{\otimes t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{1},\dots,x_{t}\in[N]}(-1)^{f(x_{1})\,\oplus\,\cdots\,\oplus\,f(x_{t})}\outerproduct*{
x_1,
\dots,
x_t
}{
x_1,
\dots,
x_t
}

(6.94)

\displaystyle=\sum_{x_{1},\dots,x_{t}\in[N]}(-1)^{\vec{f}\,\cdot\,\left(m_{2}(x_{1})\,\oplus\,\cdots\,\oplus\,m_{2}(x_{t})\right)}\outerproduct*{
x_1,
\dots,
x_t
}{
x_1,
\dots,
x_t
}

(6.95)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\boldsymbol{x}\in[N]^{t}}(-1)^{\vec{f}\,\cdot\,\left(m_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})\right)}\outerproduct*{
\boldsymbol{x}
}{
\boldsymbol{x}
}\,.

(6.96)

where
\boldsymbol{x}\coloneqq(x_{1},\dots,x_{t})
, and
m_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})\coloneqq m_{2}(x_{1})\oplus\dots\oplus m_{2}(x_{t})
is the count mod 2 of
\boldsymbol{x}
.

By extending it linearly, we can view the tensor power representation

\rho(f)^{\otimes t}

as a representation of the corresponding group algebra
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{N}]
: For each algebra element

a\in\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{N}]
,
where

a=\sum_{f\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{N}}\;a_{f}\;f
,
we have that

\displaystyle R(a)\coloneqq\sum_{f\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{N}}\;a_{f}\;\rho(f)^{\otimes t}\,.

(6.97)

Now consider the Fourier basis of the group algebra:

\displaystyle\bigg\{\hat{\mu}\coloneqq\frac{1}{2^{N}}\sum_{f\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{N}}(-1)^{\vec{f}\,\cdot\,\vec{\mu}}\;f\bigg\}_{\mu\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{N}}\,.

(6.98)

The representations
R(\hat{\mu})
of the Fourier basis form another basis for the span of the tensor power operators. We can compute that

\displaystyle R(\hat{\mu})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{N}}\sum_{f\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{N}}(-1)^{\vec{f}\,\cdot\,\vec{\mu}}\;\rho(f)^{\otimes t}

(6.99)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{N}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{N}\\
\boldsymbol{x}\in[N]^{t}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\vec{f}\,\cdot\,\left(\vec{\mu}\;\oplus\;m_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})\right)}\outerproduct*{
\boldsymbol{x}
}{
\boldsymbol{x}
}

(6.100)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{x}\in[N]^{t}\\
m_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})=\vec{\mu}\end{subarray}}\outerproduct*{
\boldsymbol{x}
}{
\boldsymbol{x}
}

(6.101)

Note that

m_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})

always has Hamming weight at most
t
,
so this is
0
whenever
\mu
has Hamming weight larger than
t
.
Otherwise, if
|\mu|\leq t
we interpret
\mu
as a subset of
[N]
and observe that
R(\hat{\mu})
is a projector onto strings
x_{1},\dots,x_{t}
such that each
x\in\mu
appears an odd number of times in the string and each
x\notin\mu
appears an even number (potentially zero times).
Note further that the subspaces corresponding to these projectors are mutually orthogonal:
R(\hat{\mu})R(\hat{\nu})=\delta_{\mu\nu}R(\hat{\mu})
.

Their commutant
\mathcal{A}_{t}
is therefore the algebra of operators that preserve these subspaces, and is spanned by operators of the form

\displaystyle\Big\{E^{\mu}_{\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}}\coloneqq\outerproduct{
\boldsymbol{a}
}{
\boldsymbol{b}
}\;\Big|\;m_{2}(\boldsymbol{a})=m_{2}(\boldsymbol{b})=\mu\Big\}_{\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}\in[N]^{t},\,\mu\subseteq[N],}

(6.102)

This basis of the commutant algebra already presents it as a direct sum of matrix algebras. That is, this is the Fourier basis of the algebra. We can thus directly see that the irreps of this algebra are parameterized by
\mu\subseteq[N]
such that
|\mu|\leq t
, and furthermore, that the dimension of irrep
\mu
is the total number of ways to choose
\boldsymbol{a}\in[N]^{t}
such that
m_{2}(a)=\mu
:

\displaystyle\dim(V^{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t_{1},\dots,t_{N}\geq 0\\
t_{i}\text{ odd iff }i\in\mu\\
\sum_{i}t_{i}=t\end{subarray}}\frac{t!}{t_{1}!\dots t_{N}!}

(6.103)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{t}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{N},\,\,|\,\vec{t}\,|=t\\
\vec{\mu}=\vec{t}\mod 2\end{subarray}}\frac{t!}{t_{1}!\dots t_{N}!}\,.

(6.104)

By
Lemma

3.31
,
Item

1
,
we have that

S\bigg(\Big(E^{\mu}_{\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}}\Big)^{*}\bigg)^{T}=\frac{1}{\dim(V^{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}})}S\Big(E^{\mu}_{\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}}\Big)
.

We thus have that the reweighting and symmetrization operators have the following form:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\subseteq[N],\,\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}\in[N]^{t}\\
m_{2}(\boldsymbol{a})=m_{2}(\boldsymbol{b})=\mu\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\dim(V^{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}})}\;S\Big(E^{\mu}_{\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}}\Big)\otimes S\Big(E^{\mu}_{\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}}\Big)

(6.105)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}\in[N]^{t}\\
m_{2}(\boldsymbol{a})=m_{2}(\boldsymbol{b})\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\dim\left(V^{m_{2}(\boldsymbol{a})}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}\right)}\;\outerproduct{
\boldsymbol{a}
}{
\boldsymbol{b}
}\otimes\outerproduct{
\boldsymbol{a}
}{
\boldsymbol{b}
}\,,

(6.106)

and as before, since we now have that the two registers are always equal in the standard basis, we will abuse notation and write it as if it were a single register

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}\in[N]^{t}\\
m_{2}(\boldsymbol{a})=m_{2}(\boldsymbol{b})\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\dim\left(V^{m_{2}(\boldsymbol{a})}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}\right)}\;\outerproduct{
\boldsymbol{a}
}{
\boldsymbol{b}
}\,.

(6.107)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\subseteq[N]\end{subarray}}\sqrt{\frac{\dim(V^{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}})}{\dim(V^{\mu}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{N}})}}\;\Pi_{\mu}

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}^{+}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\subseteq[N]\end{subarray}}\sqrt{\frac{\dim(V^{\mu}_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{N}})}{\dim(V^{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}})}}\;\Pi_{\mu}

(6.108)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\subseteq[N]\end{subarray}}\sqrt{\dim(V^{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}})}\;\Pi_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\subseteq[N]\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\dim(V^{\mu}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}})}}\Pi_{\mu}

(6.109)

where

\;\Pi_{\mu}\coloneqq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{x}\in[N]^{t},\,m_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})=\vec{\mu}\end{subarray}}\outerproduct*{
\boldsymbol{x}
}{
\boldsymbol{x}
}

are the projectors from
Equation

6.101
,
and the second line follows since
\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{N}
is an Abelian group, so its irreps are all 1-dimensional.

For
\boldsymbol{x}\in[N]^{t}
, let

\{\boldsymbol{x}\}_{\textrm{mod }2}\subseteq[N]

be the set corresponding to
m_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})
. That is, it is the set of values appearing an odd number of times in
\boldsymbol{x}
.
Let

\displaystyle\ket{
\{
\boldsymbol{x}
\}_{\textrm{mod } 2}
}\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\dim\left(V^{m_{2}(\boldsymbol{a})}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}\right)}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{y}\in[N]^{t}\\
m_{2}(\boldsymbol{y})=m_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})\end{subarray}}\ket{\boldsymbol{y}}

(6.110)

be the uniform superposition over strings of the same parity.

We then have that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}\in[N]^{t}\\
m_{2}(\boldsymbol{a})=m_{2}(\boldsymbol{b})\end{subarray}}\sqrt{\dim\left(V^{m_{2}(\boldsymbol{a})}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}\right)}\;\frac{1}{\dim\left(V^{m_{2}(\boldsymbol{a})}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}\right)}\;\outerproduct{
\boldsymbol{a}
}{
\boldsymbol{b}
}

(6.111)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}\in[N]^{t}\\
m_{2}(\boldsymbol{a})=m_{2}(\boldsymbol{b})\end{subarray}}\;\frac{1}{\sqrt{\dim\left(V^{m_{2}(\boldsymbol{a})}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}\right)}}\;\outerproduct{
\boldsymbol{a}
}{
\boldsymbol{b}
}

(6.112)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{x}\in[N]^{t}\end{subarray}}\lvert\{\boldsymbol{x}\}_{\textrm{mod }2}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{x}\rvert\,,

(6.113)

and similarly,

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{t-1}}\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{t-1}}^{+}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{b}\in[N]^{t-1}\\
m_{2}(\boldsymbol{a})=m_{2}(\boldsymbol{b})\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\dim\left(V^{m_{2}(\boldsymbol{a})}_{\mathcal{A}_{t-1}}\right)}}\;\frac{1}{\dim\left(V^{m_{2}(\boldsymbol{a})}_{\mathcal{A}_{t-1}}\right)}\;\outerproduct{
\boldsymbol{a}
}{
\boldsymbol{b}
}

(6.114)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{x}\in[N]^{t-1}\end{subarray}}\;\frac{1}{\dim\left(V^{m_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})}_{\mathcal{A}_{t-1}}\right)}\;\lvert\boldsymbol{x}\rangle\langle\{\boldsymbol{x}\}_{\textrm{mod }2}\rvert\,.

(6.115)

Let

d_{\boldsymbol{x}}\coloneqq\frac{1}{\dim\left(V^{m_{2}(\boldsymbol{x})}_{\mathcal{A}_{t-1}}\right)}

for brevity.
As before,
we now combine these to conclude:

\displaystyle V_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{N}}

\displaystyle=\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i-1}}

(6.116)

\displaystyle=\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq{i-1}}}\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i-1}}

(by
Lemma

5.17
)

\displaystyle=\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\operatorname{App}\Big)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}\Big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq{i-1}}}\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i-1}}

(
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}
and
\operatorname{App}
act on different registers)

\displaystyle=\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\Big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\left(\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in[N]\\
\phantom{}{}^{\phantom{i-1}}\boldsymbol{x}\in[N]^{i-1}\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\outerproduct*{x}{x}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\lvert\boldsymbol{x},x\rangle_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\langle\boldsymbol{x}\,\rvert_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq{i-1}}}\right)_{\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\Big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq{i-1}}}\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{i-1}}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i-1}}

(definition of
\operatorname{App}
)

\displaystyle=\left(\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\phantom{}{}^{\phantom{i-1}}\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\in[N]^{i-1}\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\lvert\{\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\}_{\textrm{mod }2}\rangle\langle\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\rvert\right)_{\!\!\!\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\left(\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in[N]\\
\phantom{}{}^{\phantom{i-1}}\boldsymbol{x}\in[N]^{i-1}\end{subarray}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\outerproduct*{x}{x}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\lvert\boldsymbol{x},x\rangle_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\langle\boldsymbol{x}\,\rvert_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq{i-1}}}\right)_{\!\!\!\!\mathsf{A}\,\mathsf{R}_{i}}\!\!\!\!\left(\!\!\!\!\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\phantom{}{}^{\phantom{i-1}}\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime\prime}\in[N]^{i-1}\end{subarray}}\!\frac{1}{d_{\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime\prime}}}\lvert\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime\prime}\rangle\langle\{\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime\prime}\}_{\textrm{mod }2}\rvert\right)_{\!\!\!\mathsf{R}_{\leq i-1}}

(by
Equations

6.113
and
6.115
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in[N]\\
\phantom{}{}^{\phantom{i-1}}\boldsymbol{x}\in[N]^{i-1}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{d_{\boldsymbol{x}}}\outerproduct*{x}{x}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\lvert\{\boldsymbol{x}\}_{\textrm{mod }2}\oplus\{x\}\rangle_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\langle\{\boldsymbol{x}\}\rvert_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq{i-1}}}

(6.117)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in[N]\\
R\subseteq[N]\end{subarray}}\outerproduct*{x}{x}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\ket{
R
\oplus\{
x
\}
}_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\bra{
R\,
}_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq{i-1}}}

(6.118)

Where the last equality follows since each multiset
R=\{\boldsymbol{x}\}_{\textrm{mod }2}
corresponds to

d_{\boldsymbol{x}}

distinct strings. This completes the proof.
∎

It turns out that there is another characterization of this commutant algebra in terms of colored even-partition diagrams.

Definition 6.17

(Colored Even-Partition Algebra)
.

The
even-partition algebra
is the algebra of partition diagrams in which every component has even number of vertices.
This is equivalent to the Tanabe algebra
T_{t}(N,2)

[
Tan97
]
.

The
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
-
colored even-partition algebra
is the algebra that has a basis of even-partition diagrams with each component assigned a color in
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
(see
Figure

17
for an example of such a colored diagram).
52
52

52

This diagram algebra is closely related to the
G
-colored partition algebras studied by
[
Blo03
]
.

Diagram multiplication accumulates the colors of each of the components that get merged together.

f_{1}
f_{2}
f_{3}
f_{4}

(6.119)

Figure 17
:
Colored even-partition diagram on
8
elements with colors
f_{1},f_{2},f_{3},f_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}_{K}
attached to the four components.

The Schur representation on
(\mathbb{C}^{N})^{\otimes t}
differs from that of the partition algebra by attaching a phase of
\omega_{N}^{f_{i}x_{i}}
to the sum over
x_{i}
of a component of color
f_{i}

(similar to Schur representation of
\mathbb{Z}_{K}\wr S_{t}
on
(\mathbb{C}^{KN})^{\otimes t}
).
