(6.120)

Lemma 6.18
.

The commutant algebra of
\rho(f)^{\otimes t}

is isomorphic to
the
colored even-partition algebra
.

Proof sketch.

This comes from simply performing a change of basis on the basis of
Equation

6.102
in much the same way as in
Equation

6.46
.
∎

7
PC is a Pseudorandom Unitary

In this section, we prove that any algorithm making
t
queries cannot distinguish a Haar-random unitary from a random Clifford circuit (or arbitrary
2
-design) composed with a random permutation with advantage better than
O(t^{2}/N)
. Throughout this section, we assume
t\leq c\sqrt{N}
for a sufficiently small
absolute constant
c>0
. In particular, this guarantees that the partition algebra
P_{t}(N)
is semisimple and all
O(t^{2}/N)
error terms below are smaller than an absolute constant.

In order to prove this, we carefully analyze the path-recording oracles
Q_{U(N)}^{\mathrm{Path}}
,
Q_{S_{N}}^{\mathrm{Path}}
for the defining representation of
U(N)
and the in-place representation of
S_{N}
. In particular, we prove that they are close in trace distance on the distinct, nonplussed subspace
\mathsf{DNP}
(
Definition

7.5
), and prove that a random
2
-design
C
causes almost all of the recording state to land in
\mathsf{DNP}
. Proving the first of these statements, in turn, requires two main steps: an analysis of the dual elements of permutations
[\pi]^{*}\in P_{t}(N)
within the partition algebra, and an analysis of
\lambda
-irrep dimensions in
(U(N),S_{t})
and
(S_{N},P_{t}(N))
Schur-Weyl duality.

7.1
The Distinct, Nonplussed Subspace

7.1.1
Preliminaries

Lemma 7.1

(
[
MPSY24
,
MH25
]
)
.

Let
C
be any unitary
2
-design on
\mathbb{C}^{N}
. Then,

\mathbb{E}_{C}\!\left[\Tr\left(\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}}C^{\otimes t}\rho(C^{\dagger})^{\otimes t}\right)\right]\geq 1-\frac{t(t-1)}{N+1}

(7.1)

Definition 7.2
.

Let
\ket{+}
denote the uniform superposition on the standard basis of
\mathbb{C}^{N}
, and define
W=\mathbf{I}-\outerproduct{+}{+}
, i.e. the projector away from
\ket{+}
. For a system of
t
registers, the
nonplussed subspace
53
53

53

In other words, we are completely unfazed by states in this subspace.

\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}=W^{\otimes t}
is obtained by projecting away the uniform superposition on each register.

An orthonormal basis for
\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}
is given by

\left\{\frac{1}{{N}^{t/2}}\sum_{\vec{x}\in[N]^{t}}\omega_{N}^{\left\langle{\vec{x},\vec{y}}\right\rangle}\ket{\vec x}\right\}_{(y_{1},\dots,y_{t})\in\left(\mathbb{Z}_{N}\setminus\{0\}\right)^{t}}

(7.2)

Another basis for
\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}
that will often be convenient for us (but not orthonormal) is given by

\left\{\bigotimes_{i=1}^{t}\big(\ket{1}-\ket{x_i}\big)\right\}_{(x_{1},\dots,x_{t})\in\left([N]\setminus\{1\}\right)^{t}}

(7.3)

Note that, while not orthonormal, these basis states are linearly independent, since only the state corresponding to
\vec{x}
has support on
\ket{\vec x}
.

Lemma 7.3
.

Let
C
be sampled from any unitary
1
-design on
\mathbb{C}^{N}
. Then,

\mathbb{E}_{C}\!\left[\Tr\left(\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}}C^{\otimes t}\rho(C^{\dagger})^{\otimes t}\right)\right]\geq 1-\frac{t}{N}

(7.4)

Proof.

Since
\{\outerproduct{+}{+}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{t}
are commuting projectors, we can use the operator form of the union bound:

Lemma 7.4

(
[
KOMW19
]
)
.

Let
\rho
be a density operator, and let

\Pi_{1},\ldots,\Pi_{m}
be commuting orthogonal projectors. Then

1-\operatorname{Tr}\left(\Pi_{m}\cdots\Pi_{1}\rho\Pi_{1}\cdots\Pi_{m}\right)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{m}\operatorname{Tr}\left((\mathbf{I}-\Pi_{i})\rho\right).

(7.5)

which implies that

W^{\otimes t}=(\mathbf{I}-\outerproduct{+}{+})^{\otimes t}\succeq\mathbf{I}-\sum_{i=1}^{t}\outerproduct{+}{+}

(7.6)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\Tr( \Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}}C^{\otimes t} \rho(C^\dagger)^{\otimes t})\geq 1-\sum_{i=1}^{t}\Tr(\ketbra{+}{+}_iC^{\otimes t} \rho(C^\dagger)^{\otimes t})

(7.7)

\displaystyle\implies\mathbb{E}_{C}\!\left[\Tr\left(\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}}C^{\otimes t}\rho(C^{\dagger})^{\otimes t}\right)\right]\geq 1-\sum_{i=1}^{t}\mathbb{E}_{C}\!\left[\Tr(\ketbra{+}{+}_iC^{\otimes t} \rho(C^\dagger)^{\otimes t})\right]

(7.8)

Now, consider the summand on the right hand side. By the cyclic property of trace and linearity of expectation, the summand on the right hand side is equivalent to

\Tr( \mathbb{E}_C[(C^\dagger)^{\otimes t}\ketbra{+}{+}_iC^{\otimes t} \rho])=\Tr( \mathbb{E}_C[(C^\dagger\ketbra{+}{+}C)_i] \rho)

(7.9)

For any
1
-design,
\mathbb{E}_{C}[C^{\dagger}\sigma C]=\frac{\Tr(\sigma)}{N}\mathbf{I}
. Since
\sigma=\outerproduct{+}{+}
has trace
1
,
Eq.

7.9
simplifies to
\Tr(\rho)/N=1/N
. Plugging this into the summand in
Eq.

7.8
completes the proof.
∎

Definition 7.5
.

The
distinct nonplussed subspace

\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}:=\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}\cap\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}
is the intersection of the distinct and nonplussed subspaces.

Lemma 7.6
.

The distinct nonplussed subspace
\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}
has dimension

N^{t}\left(1-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)\right)
.
In particular, this means that the fully mixed state has probability
at least

1-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)

of being in the distinct nonplussed subspace.

Proof.

We can take the following to be a partial and non-orthogonal basis for the distinct nonplussed subspace:

S=\left\{\bigotimes_{i=1}^{t}\big(\ket{i}-\ket{x_i}\big)\right\}_{(x_{1},\dots,x_{t})\in\left([N]\setminus\{1,...,t\}\right)^{t}_{\text{dist}}}

(7.10)

As in
Equation

7.3
, these basis states are linearly independent (though not orthogonal) because only the state corresponding to
\vec{x}
has support on
\ket{\vec x}
.
Furthermore,
S
is contained in the distinct nonplussed subspace:
Fix
\ket{\psi}\in S
. Clearly,
\ket{\psi}\in\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}
, since all
t
registers are supported on pairwise disjoint sets of computational basis vectors. Moreover,

\displaystyle W(\ket{i}-\ket{x_i})

\displaystyle=\ket{i}-\ket{x_i}+\left(\langle+|i\rangle-\langle+|x_{i}\rangle\right)\ket{+}

(7.11)

\displaystyle=\ket{i}-\ket{x_i}+\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}-\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\right)\ket{+}

(7.12)

\displaystyle=\ket{i}-\ket{x_i}

(7.13)

\displaystyle\implies W^{\otimes t}\ket{\psi}=\ket{\psi}

(7.14)

and so
\ket{\psi}\in\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}
as well.
So the size of
S
is a lower bound on the dimension of the distinct nonplussed subspace.
This gives us that

\displaystyle\dim(\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t})\geq|S|

\displaystyle=(N-t)(N-t-1)\dots(N-2t+1)

(7.15)

\displaystyle\geq N^{t}\left(1-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)\right).\qed

(7.16)

7.1.2
Unitary 2-designs and the Distinct Nonplussed Subspace

In this section, we will show that applying a unitary
2
-design with high probability maps an arbitrary state
\rho\in(\mathbb{C}^{N})^{\otimes t}
to a state that has high overlap with the distinct nonplussed subspace. More precisely, we will prove the following theorem:

Theorem 7.7
.

Let
\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}}
be the projection onto the distinct nonplussed subspace, and
C
be any unitary
2
-design on
\mathbb{C}^{N}
. Then,

\mathbb{E}_{C}\!\left[\Tr\!\left(\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}}C^{\otimes t}\rho(C^{\dagger})^{\otimes t}\right)\right]\geq 1-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)

(7.17)

for any state
\rho\in(\mathbb{C}^{N})^{\otimes t}
.

To prove
Theorem

7.7
, we first collect some facts about projectors:

Lemma 7.8

(
[
Gao15
]
)
.

Let
\rho
be a density operator, and let

\Pi_{1},\ldots,\Pi_{m}
be orthogonal projectors. Then

1-\operatorname{Tr}\left(\Pi_{m}\cdots\Pi_{1}\rho\Pi_{1}\cdots\Pi_{m}\right)\leq 4\sum_{i=1}^{m}\operatorname{Tr}\left((\mathbf{I}-\Pi_{i})\rho\right).

(7.18)

Equivalently,

\operatorname{Tr}\left(\Pi_{m}\cdots\Pi_{1}\rho\Pi_{1}\cdots\Pi_{m}\right)\geq 1-4\sum_{i=1}^{m}\operatorname{Tr}\left((\mathbf{I}-\Pi_{i})\rho\right).

(7.19)

Definition 7.9
.

Assume that
A
and
B
are subspaces of
\mathbb{C}^{N}
, and let
C\coloneqq A\cap B
be the intersection. The
Friedrichs angle

c(A,B)
is defined as

c(A,B)\coloneqq\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ket{x}\in A\cap C^{\perp}\\
\ket{y}\in B\cap C^{\perp}\\
\norm{\ket{x}}=\norm{\ket{y}}=1\end{subarray}}\absolutevalue{\inner{x|y}}

(7.20)

Informally, the Friedrichs angle is the highest overlap between two unit vectors in
A
and
B
, excluding the intersection. For example, when
\Pi_{A}
and
\Pi_{B}
are commuting projectors,
c(A,B)=0
. Notice that since
\ket{y}\in B\cap C^{\perp}
,

\displaystyle\norm{\Pi_A\ket{y}}=\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ket{a}\in A\\
\norm{\ket{a}}=1\end{subarray}}|\langle a|y\rangle|

\displaystyle=\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ket{a}\in A\cap C^{\perp}\\
\norm{\ket{a}}=1\end{subarray}}|\langle a|y\rangle|

(7.21)

\displaystyle\implies\norm{\Pi_A\mid_{B \cap C^\perp}\!}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ket{y}\in B\cap C^{\perp}\\
\norm{\ket{y}}=1\end{subarray}}|\langle y|\Pi_{A}|y\rangle|

(7.22)

\displaystyle=\sup\limits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ket{x}\in A\cap C^{\perp}\\
\ket{y}\in B\cap C^{\perp}\\
\norm{\ket{x}}=\norm{\ket{y}}=1\end{subarray}}|\langle y|x\rangle\langle x|y\rangle|=c(A,B)^{2}

(7.23)

We make use of two more characterizations of
c(A,B)
. First,

Claim 7.10
.

c(A,B)
is the largest singular value of
\Pi_{A}\Pi_{B}
aside from
1
.

Proof.

Since
A
decomposes into
C\oplus(A\cap C^{\bot})
, we have that

\Pi_{A}\Pi_{B}=\Pi_{C}\Pi_{B}+\Pi_{A\cap C^{\bot}}\Pi_{B}=\Pi_{C}+\Pi_{A\cap C^{\bot}}\Pi_{B}.

Moreover, decomposing
B=C\oplus(B\cap C^{\bot})
, we can write

\Pi_{A}\Pi_{B}=\Pi_{C}+\Pi_{A\cap C^{\bot}}\Pi_{C}+\Pi_{A\cap C^{\bot}}\Pi_{B\cap C^{\bot}}=\Pi_{C}+\Pi_{A\cap C^{\bot}}\Pi_{B\cap C^{\bot}}.

Thus,
\Pi_{A}\Pi_{B}
block-diagonalizes according to the
(C,A\cap C^{\bot})
,
(C,B\cap C^{\bot})
vector space decompositions.
c(A,B)
is defined to be the top singular value of the
(A\cap C^{\bot},B\cap C^{\bot})
block, so this proves the claim.
∎

Kayalar and Weinert proved that the Friedrichs angle captures the difference between first projecting onto
A
, then projecting onto
B
, as opposed to a single projection onto
A\cap B
:

Theorem 7.11

(
[
KW89
]
)
.

||\Pi_{A}\Pi_{B}-\Pi_{A\cap B}||=c(A,B).

(7.24)

From
Theorem

7.11
, we can derive a related bound which will be useful in our setting:

Lemma 7.12
.

\norm{\Pi_B\Pi_A\Pi_B - \Pi_{A \cap B}}\leq c(A,B)^{2}

(7.25)

Proof.

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Pi_{B}\Pi_{A}\Pi_{B}-\Pi_{A\cap B}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\Pi_{B}\Pi_{A}\Pi_{A}\Pi_{B}-\Pi_{A\cap B}\right\rVert

(since
\Pi_{A}^{2}=\Pi_{A}
)

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\Pi_{B}\Pi_{A}\Pi_{A}\Pi_{B}-\Pi_{A\cap B}-\Pi_{A\cap B}+\Pi_{A\cap B}\right\rVert

(7.26)

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\Pi_{B}\Pi_{A}\Pi_{A}\Pi_{B}-\Pi_{B}\Pi_{A}\Pi_{A\cap B}-\Pi_{A\cap B}\Pi_{A}\Pi_{B}+\Pi_{A\cap B}\right\rVert

(since
\Pi_{A}\Pi_{A\cap B}=\Pi_{A\cap B}
)

\displaystyle=\left\lVert(\Pi_{B}\Pi_{A}-\Pi_{A\cap B})(\Pi_{A}\Pi_{B}-\Pi_{A\cap B})\right\rVert

(7.27)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\Pi_{B}\Pi_{A}-\Pi_{A\cap B}\right\rVert\;\left\lVert\Pi_{A}\Pi_{B}-\Pi_{A\cap B}\right\rVert

(submultiplicativity)

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\Pi_{A}\Pi_{B}-\Pi_{A\cap B}\right\rVert^{2}

(7.28)

\displaystyle\leq c(A,B)^{2}

(
Theorem

7.11
)

∎

Corollary 7.13
.

\displaystyle\left\lvert\Tr(\Pi_A\Pi_B\rho\Pi_B\Pi_A)-\Tr(\Pi_{A \cap B}\rho\Pi_{A \cap B})\right\rvert\leq c(A,B)^{2}.

(7.29)

Proof.

\displaystyle\left\lvert\Tr(\Pi_A\Pi_B\rho\Pi_B\Pi_A)-\Tr(\Pi_{A \cap B}\rho\Pi_{A \cap B})\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\Tr(\Pi_B\Pi_A\Pi_A\Pi_B\rho)-\Tr(\Pi_{A \cap B}\Pi_{A \cap B}\rho)\right\rvert

(7.30)

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\Tr(\Pi_B\Pi_A\Pi_B\rho)-\Tr(\Pi_{A \cap B}\rho)\right\rvert

(7.31)

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\Tr\big((\Pi_B\Pi_A\Pi_B - \Pi_{A \cap B})\rho\big)\right\rvert

(7.32)

\displaystyle\leq||\Pi_{B}\Pi_{A}\Pi_{B}-\Pi_{A\cap B}||\;\norm{\rho}_{1}

(Hölder’s inequality)

\displaystyle=||\Pi_{B}\Pi_{A}\Pi_{B}-\Pi_{A\cap B}||

(
\norm{\rho}_{1}
= 1)

\displaystyle=c(A,B)^{2}

(
Lemma

7.12
)

∎

Finally, we will make use of one more fact about the Friedrichs angle:

Lemma 7.14
.

\Pi_{A}\Pi_{B}+\Pi_{B}\Pi_{A}\succeq-c(A,B)\Big(\Pi_{A}+\Pi_{B}\Big)

Proof.

By Jordan’s lemma,
\Pi_{A}
and
\Pi_{B}
are simultaneously block-diagonalizable into one- and two-dimensional subspaces where on the two dimensional subspaces we have

\Pi_{A}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix},\qquad\Pi_{B}=\begin{pmatrix}\cos^{2}\theta&\cos\theta\sin\theta\\
\cos\theta\sin\theta&\sin^{2}\theta\end{pmatrix}

for some angle
0<\theta<\frac{\pi}{2}
. The one-dimensional subspaces together span
A\cap B
,
A^{\bot}\cap B
,
A\cap B^{\bot}
, and
A^{\bot}\cap B^{\bot}
. Thus, in this language, the Friedrichs angle is given by

c(A,B)=\max\cos(\theta),

where the maximum is taken over two-dimensional Jordan blocks. But on any fixed Jordan block, one can see that

\Pi_{A}\Pi_{B}+\Pi_{B}\Pi_{A}+\cos(\theta)(\Pi_{A}+\Pi_{B})=\cos(\theta)\cdot\begin{pmatrix}(1+\cos(\theta))^{2}&\sin\theta(1+\cos(\theta))\\
\sin\theta(1+\cos(\theta))&\sin^{2}\theta\end{pmatrix}\succeq 0,

which proves the claim.
∎

Our first goal will be to show that
c({\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}},{\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}})^{2}
is small when
t\ll N
:

Lemma 7.15
.

c({\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}},{\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}})^{2}\leq\frac{t(t-1)}{N-t+1}.

(7.33)

Proof.

Define the averaging operator
A_{i}
as follows:

A_{i}\ket{\vec{x}}=\begin{cases}\dfrac{1}{N-t+1}\displaystyle\sum_{a\notin\{x_{j}:\,j\neq i\}}\ket{x_1, \dots, x_{i-1}, a, x_{i+1}, \dots, x_t}&\text{if }\vec{x}\in[N]_{\text{dist}}^{t},\\[8.61108pt]
\ket{\vec{x}},&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

(7.34)

From the definition, it is clear that
A_{i}
is an orthogonal projector, whose normalized
+1
eigenvectors within the distinct subspace are given by

\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-t+1}}\sum_{a\notin\{z_{j}:\,j\neq i\}}\ket{z_1, \dots, z_{i-1}, a, z_{i+1}, \dots, z_t},\;\;\vec{z}=(z_{1},\dots,z_{i-1},z_{i+1},\dots,z_{t})\in[N]_{\text{dist}}^{t-1}

(7.35)

and a (overcomplete) basis for
\ker A_{i}
(which is within the distinct subspace) is given by

\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\ket{z_1, \dots, z_{i-1}, a, z_{i+1} \dots, z_t}-\ket{z_1, \dots, z_{i-1}, b, z_{i+1} \dots, z_t}),\;\;a\neq b

(7.36)

We will be interested in the (Hermitian, PSD) operator

A=\sum_{i}A_{i}.

We first show that the kernel of
A
is exactly the intersection
\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}\cap\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}
:

Claim 7.16
.

\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}\cap\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}=\bigcap_{i=1}^{t}\ker A_{i}=\ker A

(7.37)

Proof.

We know that
\bigcap_{i=1}^{t}\ker A_{i}=\ker A
because the
A_{i}
are all PSD.

For the main claim, by definition of
\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}
,
\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}=\bigcap_{i=1}^{t}\im W^{(i)}
.
So it is sufficient for us to show that for each
i
,

\ker A_{i}=\mathsf{Dist}\cap\im W^{(i)}

(7.38)

For the forward inclusion, it is clear by definition of
A_{i}
that
\ker A_{i}\subseteq\mathsf{Dist}
. Moreover, for any state
\ket{\phi}
in
Eq.

7.36
,
\Tr_{[t]\setminus i}(\outerproduct{\phi}{\phi})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}(\ket{a}-\ket{b})
is orthogonal to
\ket{+}
, and therefore in
\im W
. Thus

\ker A_{i}\subseteq\mathsf{Dist}\cap\im W^{(i)}.

(7.39)

Conversely, if
\ket{\psi}\notin\ker A_{i}
, then either
\ket{\psi}
has support on a non-distinct
t
-tuple (in which case
\ket{\psi}\notin\mathsf{Dist}
), or has support on a basis state
\ket{\phi}
in
Eq.

7.35
, which satisfies

\Tr_{[t]\setminus i}(\outerproduct{\phi}{\phi})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-t+1}}\sum_{a\notin\{z_{j}:\,j\neq i\}}\ket{a}

(7.40)

which has nonzero overlap with
\ket{+}\in\ker W
. Hence,
\ket{\phi}\notin\im W^{(i)}
, and so

\ket{\psi}\notin\ker A_{i}\implies\ket{\psi}\notin\mathsf{Dist}\cap\im W^{(i)}.

(7.41)

∎

Next, we calculate the pairwise Friedrichs angles
c(\im(A_{i}),\im(A_{j}))
for
i\neq j
.

Claim 7.17
.

c(\im(A_{i}),\im(A_{j}))\leq\frac{1}{N-t+1}
for all
i<j
.

Proof.

The projections
A_{i}
and
A_{j}
are simultaneously block-diagonal in the coordinates
\ell\notin\{i,j\}
. Thus, it suffices to prove the claim restricting to states of the

\bigotimes_{\ell\notin\{i,j\}}\ket{x_\ell}\otimes\ket{\psi_{x_{\neq i,j}}}

where
\ket{\psi_{x_{\neq i,j}}}
is on the
i
th and
j
th registers. Restricted to these states and looking only at registers
i
and
j
,
\im(A_{i})
is spanned by states of the form

\ket{\psi_{x_j}^{(i)}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-t+1}}\sum_{x^{\prime}_{i}\notin\{x_{\ell}\}_{\ell\neq i}}\ket{x'_i}\ket{x_j}

for all
x_{j}\notin\{x_{\ell}\}_{\ell\notin\{i,j\}}
, while
\im(A_{j})
is similarly spanned by

\ket{\psi_{x_i}^{(j)}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N-t+1}}\sum_{x^{\prime}_{j}\notin\{x_{\ell}\}_{\ell\neq j}}\ket{x_i}\ket{x'_j}

for all
x_{i}\notin\{x_{\ell}\}_{\ell\notin\{i,j\}}
. The corresponding Gram matrix of all such inner products
\innerproduct{\psi_{x_i}^{(j)}}{\psi_{x_j}^{(i)}}
is given by

\frac{1}{N-t+1}\Big(J_{N-t+2}-\mathbf{I}_{N-t+2}\Big),

where
J
denotes an all ones matrix, as the diagonal terms are indeed zero while the off-diagonal terms are
\frac{1}{N-t+1}
. By
7.10
, the
c(\im(A_{i}),\im(A_{j}))
is the largest singular value of this matrix aside from
1
. Because
J
has rank
1
, all remaining singular values of the Gram matrix are
\frac{1}{N-t+1}
, so the claim follows.
∎

In order to finish proving
Lemma

7.15
, we will need to establish one more key claim.

Claim 7.18
.

A\succeq(1-\frac{t-1}{N-t+1})(\mathbf{I}-\Pi_{\ker(A)})
.

Proof.

By
7.17
and
Lemma

7.14
, we have that

A_{i}A_{j}+A_{j}A_{i}\succeq-\frac{1}{N-t+1}\Big(A_{i}+A_{j}\Big)

for all
i\neq j
. Thus, we can calculate

\displaystyle A^{2}

\displaystyle=A+\sum_{i<j}(A_{i}A_{j}+A_{j}A_{i})

(7.42)

\displaystyle\succeq A-\frac{1}{N-t+1}\sum_{i<j}(A_{i}+A_{j})

(7.43)

\displaystyle=(1-\frac{t-1}{N-t+1})A.

(7.44)

Since
A
is Hermitian PSD, this means that all nonzero eigenvalues of
A
are at least
(1-\frac{t-1}{N-t+1})
and so

A\succeq(1-\frac{t-1}{N-t+1})(\mathbf{I}-\Pi_{\ker A}).\qed

We are finally ready to prove
Lemma

7.15
. Fix any
\ket{\psi}\in\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}\cap(\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t})^{\perp}
, and define
\ket{\phi}\coloneqq\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}}\ket{\psi}
and
\ket{\eta}\coloneqq(\mathbf{I}-\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}})\ket{\psi}
. By
Eq.

7.23
, bounding
c({\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}},{\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}})^{2}
reduces to bounding

\langle\psi|\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}}|\psi\rangle=\langle\phi|\phi\rangle=\langle\phi|\mathbf{I}-\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}}|\phi\rangle\leq\frac{N-t+1}{N-2t+2}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\langle\phi|A_{i}|\phi\rangle=\frac{N-t+1}{N-2t+2}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\norm{A_i\ket{\phi}}^{2}

(7.45)

where the second equality holds because
\ket{\phi}
is orthogonal to
\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}
: for any
\ket{\gamma}\in\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}
,

\langle\gamma|\phi\rangle=\langle\gamma|\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}}|\psi\rangle=\langle\gamma|\psi\rangle=0

(7.46)

Now, we turn to bounding
\norm{A_i\ket{\phi}}^{2}
. For any
\vec{z}\in[N]^{t-1}
and
a\in[N]
, let
\alpha_{\vec{z},a}
be the coefficient of
\ket{z_1, \dots, z_{i-1}, a, z_{i+1}, \dots, z_t}
when expanding
\ket{\psi}
in the computational basis. Since
\ket{\psi}\in\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}
,

\sum_{a\in[N]}\alpha_{\vec{z},a}=0\implies-\sum_{a\in\{z_{j}:j\neq i\}}\alpha_{\vec{z},a}=\sum_{a\notin\{z_{j}:j\neq i\}}\alpha_{\vec{z},a}

(7.47)

But then,

\displaystyle A_{i}\ket{\phi}

\displaystyle=A_{i}\left(\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}}\ket{\psi}\right)

(7.48)

\displaystyle=A_{i}\left(\sum_{\vec{z}\in[N]^{t-1}_{\text{dist}}}\sum_{a\notin\{z_{j}:j\neq i\}}\alpha_{\vec{z},a}\ket{z_1, \dots, z_{i-1}, a, z_{i+1}, \dots, z_t}\right)

(7.49)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N-t+1}\sum_{\vec{z}\in[N]^{t-1}_{\text{dist}}}\sum_{b\notin\{z_{j}:j\neq i\}}\sum_{a\notin\{z_{j}:j\neq i\}}\alpha_{\vec{z},a}\ket{z_1, \dots, z_{i-1}, b, z_{i+1}, \dots, z_t}

(7.50)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{N-t+1}\sum_{\vec{z}\in[N]^{t-1}_{\text{dist}}}\sum_{b\notin\{z_{j}:j\neq i\}}\sum_{a\in\{z_{j}:j\neq i\}}\alpha_{\vec{z},a}\ket{z_1, \dots, z_{i-1}, b, z_{i+1}, \dots, z_t}

(7.51)

\displaystyle\implies\norm{A_i\ket{\phi}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(N-t+1)^{2}}\sum_{\vec{z}\in[N]^{t-1}_{\text{dist}}}\sum_{b\notin\{z_{j}:j\neq i\}}\left|\sum_{a\in\{z_{j}:j\neq i\}}\alpha_{\vec{z},a}\right|^{2}

(7.52)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N-t+1}\sum_{\vec{z}\in[N]^{t-1}_{\text{dist}}}\left|\sum_{a\in\{z_{j}:j\neq i\}}\alpha_{\vec{z},a}\right|^{2}

(7.53)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{t-1}{N-t+1}\sum_{\vec{z}\in[N]^{t-1}_{\text{dist}}}\sum_{a\in\{z_{j}:j\neq i\}}\left|\alpha_{\vec{z},a}\right|^{2}

(7.54)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{t-1}{N-t+1}\norm{\ket{\eta}}^{2}=\frac{t-1}{N-t+1}\left(1-\norm{\ket{\phi}}^{2}\right)

(7.55)

On the first line we expanded out the definition of
\ket{\phi}
, and on the second line we expanded out the application of
A_{i}
. On the third line, we plug in
Eq.

7.47
, the fourth is by the definition of the Euclidean norm, and the fifth uses that
\left|\{z_{j}:j\neq i\}\right|=t-1
. The sixth line follows by an application of Cauchy Schwarz, and on the last line, we use that the remaining sum is over non-distinct basis states and is therefore bounded by
\norm{\ket{\eta}}^{2}=1-\norm{\ket{\phi}}^{2}
.

We therefore conclude that

\displaystyle\norm{\ket{\phi}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{N-t+1}{N-2t+2}\sum_{i=1}^{t}\norm{A_i\ket{\phi}}^{2}

(by
Equation

7.45
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{t(t-1)}{N-2t+2}(1-\norm{\ket{\phi}}^{2})

(by
Equation

7.55
)

Rearranging gives

\norm{\ket{\phi}}^{2}\leq\frac{t(t-1)}{N-2t+2+t(t-1)}\leq\frac{t(t-1)}{N-t+1}.

Since this holds for every
\ket{\psi}
in the optimization in
Eq.

7.23
, this gives the claimed bound,
which completes the proof of
Lemma

7.15
.
∎

We can now prove
Theorem

7.7
:

Proof of
Theorem

7.7
.

First,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{C}\!\left[\Tr\left(\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}}C^{\otimes t}\rho(C^{\dagger})^{\otimes t}\right)\right]

(7.56)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{C}\!\left[\Tr\left(\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}}C^{\otimes t}\rho(C^{\dagger})^{\otimes t}\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}}\right)\right]

(7.57)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{C}\!\left[\Tr\left(\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}}\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}}C^{\otimes t}\rho(C^{\dagger})^{\otimes t}\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}}\right)\right]-c({\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}},{\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}})^{2}

(by
Corollary

7.13
)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{C}\!\left[\Tr\left(\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}}\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}}C^{\otimes t}\rho(C^{\dagger})^{\otimes t}\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}}\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}}\right)\right]-\frac{t(t-1)}{N-t+1}

(by
Lemma

7.15
)

\displaystyle\geq 1-4\cdot\mathbb{E}_{C}\!\left[\Tr\left((\mathbf{I}-\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}})C^{\otimes t}\rho(C^{\dagger})^{\otimes t}\right)\right]

(7.58)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\;4\cdot\mathbb{E}_{C}\!\left[\Tr\left((\mathbf{I}-\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}})C^{\otimes t}\rho(C^{\dagger})^{\otimes t}\right)\right]-\frac{t(t-1)}{N-t+1}

(by
Lemma

7.8
)

\displaystyle=1-4\left(1-\mathbb{E}_{C}\!\left[\Tr\left(\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}}C^{\otimes t}\rho(C^{\dagger})^{\otimes t}\right)\right]\right)

(7.59)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\;4\left(1-\mathbb{E}_{C}\!\left[\Tr\left(\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}}C^{\otimes t}\rho(C^{\dagger})^{\otimes t}\right)\right]\right)-\frac{t(t-1)}{N-t+1}

(since

\Tr\left(C^{\otimes t}\rho(C^{\dagger})^{\otimes t}\right)

=
1
)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{4t(t-1)}{N+1}-\frac{4t}{N}-\frac{t(t-1)}{N-t+1}

(by
Lemmas

7.1
and
7.3
)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{6t^{2}}{N-t+1}

(7.60)

which is
1-O(t^{2}/N)
.
∎

7.2
The Action of the Partition Algebra on the Distinct Nonplussed Subspace

Lemma 7.19
.

Let
D\in\mathcal{B}(P_{t}(N))
be a partition diagram, and let
S(\cdot)
denote the Schur representation (
Definition

3.16
) of
P_{t}(N)
.
Then

S(D)\,\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}}=0

if and only if
D
is not a permutation diagram.

Proof.

The “only if” direction is straightforward, since
S(D)
is unitary if
D
is a permutation. For the “if” direction, assuming that
D
is not a permutation diagram, there are one of two possible cases to consider:

a)

D
has a connected component with at least two vertices in the input row, and/or

b)

D
has a singleton in the input row.

This is because if no two input vertices are in the same connected component, and every input vertex is propagating (no singletons), then the input vertices must each connect to a distinct output vertex, producing a permutation.

First, assume that
D
has a connected component
C_{1}
containing two input vertices
i_{\operatorname{in}}
and
j_{\operatorname{in}}
. By definition of the Schur representation, all summands in
Eq.

3.10
will have
\bra{y_{i}}=\bra{y_j}
, which implies that
S(D)\ket{\vec{z}}=0
unless
z_{i}=z_{j}
. Therefore,
S(D)\ket{\psi}=0
for all
\ket{\psi}\in\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}
.

Next, assume that
D
has a singleton
i_{\operatorname{in}}
in the input row. Again using
Eq.

3.10
, this implies that
S(D)
factors as
V\otimes\bra{+}_{i}
, where
V:(\mathbb{C}^{N})^{\otimes t-1}\rightarrow(\mathbb{C}^{N})^{\otimes t}
.
V\otimes\bra{+}_{i}
annihilates all states that are orthogonal to
\ket{+}_{i}
, including all states of the form
\ket{\psi}\otimes(\ket{1}-\ket{z_t})
(
Eq.

7.3
). Therefore,
S(D)\ket{\psi}=0
for all
\ket{\psi}\in\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}
.
Combining the two cases, it follows that

S(D)\,\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}}=0

(7.61)

∎

Lemma 7.20
.

\mathsf{Schur}_{P_{t}(N)}
maps the distinct nonplussed subspace

\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}
to the subspace spanned by
(S_{N},P_{t}(N))
-Schur basis states
\ket{\lambda, X, Y}
with
|\lambda|=t
.

In order to prove
Lemma

7.20
, we first show that an irrep of
P_{t}(N)
which annihilates all non-permutation diagrams must correspond to a full-box irrep, i.e.
|\lambda|=t
:

Lemma 7.21
.

Let
J\trianglelefteq P_{t}(N)
be the two-sided ideal spanned by all non-permutation diagrams in
\mathcal{B}(P_{t}(N))
.
54
54

54

That
J
is in fact a two-sided ideal is a consequence of
[
HR05
]
, Equation 1.3.
Then,
\rho_{\lambda}(J)=0
if and only if
|\lambda|=t
.

Proof.

The “if” direction follows from
[
FND26
]
Theorem 4.17, which proves that if
|\lambda|=t
, then
\rho_{\lambda}(D)=0
for all diagrams
D
with fewer than
t
propagating components.
55
55

55

A connected component is
propagating
if it intersects both the input and output rows of the diagram.
The only diagrams in
\mathcal{B}(P_{t}(N))
with
t
propagating components are permutation diagrams, which implies that
\rho_{\lambda}(j)=0
for all
j\in J
.

To prove the reverse direction, note that there is a bijection between the irreps of
P_{t}(N)/J
and the irreps of
P_{t}(N)
which annihilate
J
(
[
EH18
]
, Lemma 2.37, Lemma 3.5):

\begin{array}[]{c}\{\lambda:\lambda\in\widehat{P_{t}(N)},\;\;\ \rho_{\lambda}(j)=0\;\text{for all}\;j\in J\}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\{\lambda^{\prime}:\lambda^{\prime}\in\widehat{P_{t}(N)/J}\}\end{array}

(7.62)

Since
P_{t}(N)/J\simeq\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]
(
[
HR05
]
, Equation 2.11), the number of irreducible representations of
P_{t}(N)
which annihilate
J
is equal to the number of irreducible representations of
\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]
. These are indexed by partitions of
t
. Since the
t
-box irreducible representations of
P_{t}(N)
already annihilate
J
, it follows that no other irreducible representation of
P_{t}(N)
(with
|\lambda|<t
) can annihilate
J
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

7.20
.

We begin with the forward direction, and let
j\in J
, where
J
is defined as in
Lemma

7.21
. For any
\ket{\psi}\in(\mathbb{C}^{N})^{\otimes t}
, we can write the state
S(j)\ket{\psi}
in the
(S_{N},P_{t}(N))
-Schur basis as follows:

\displaystyle\ket{\psi}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\widehat{P_{t}(N)},\\
X\in\mathcal{B}(V_{S_{N}}^{(N-|\lambda|,\lambda)}),\\
Y\in\mathcal{B}(V_{P_{t}(N)}^{\lambda})\end{subarray}}\alpha_{\lambda,X,Y}\ket{\lambda, X, Y}

(7.63)

\displaystyle\implies S(j)\ket{\psi}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\widehat{P_{t}(N)},\\
X\in\mathcal{B}(V_{S_{N}}^{(N-|\lambda|,\lambda)}),\\
Y\in\mathcal{B}(V_{P_{t}(N)}^{\lambda})\end{subarray}}\alpha_{\lambda,X,Y}\ket{\lambda, X}\otimes\rho_{\lambda}(j)\ket{Y}

(7.64)

Next, fix some
\lambda
with
|\lambda|<t
. By
Lemma

7.21
, there is some
k\in J
such that
\rho_{\lambda}(k)\neq 0
. Therefore,
\rho_{\lambda}(J)
is a non-zero ideal of
\rho_{\lambda}(A)
. But since
\lambda
is an irrep,
\rho_{\lambda}(A)
is a simple algebra, which has no non-zero proper ideals. Therefore,
\rho_{\lambda}(J)=\rho_{\lambda}(A)
, and so there exists
j_{\lambda}\in J
such that
\rho_{\lambda}(j_{\lambda})=\rho_{\lambda}(\mathbf{1})=\mathbf{I}_{V_{P_{t}(N)}^{\lambda}}
, where
\mathbf{1}
is the identity diagram in
A
.

Setting
j=j_{\lambda}
in
Eq.

7.64
implies that the coefficient on
\ket{\lambda, X, Y}
in
S(j_{\lambda})\ket{\psi}
equal to
\alpha_{\lambda,X,Y}
. But, if
\ket{\psi}\in\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}
, then
Lemma

7.19
implies that
S(j_{\lambda})\ket{\psi}=0
, so
\alpha_{\lambda,X,Y}=0
. Since
\lambda
was chosen arbitrarily among all irreps with fewer than
t
boxes, it follows that in the
(S_{N},P_{t}(N))
-Schur basis,
\ket{\psi}
is supported entirely on full-box irreps.

For the reverse direction, assume that in the
(S_{N},P_{t}(N))
-Schur basis,
\ket{\psi}
is supported entirely on states
\ket{\lambda, X, Y}
with
|\lambda|=t
. By
Lemma

7.21
,
\rho_{\lambda}(J)=0
, and so the block-diagonal decomposition on the right hand side of
Eq.

7.64
implies that
S(j)\ket{\psi}=0
for all
j\in J
. In particular,
S(B_{ij})\ket{\psi}=0
, where
B_{ij}
is the diagram with a single block containing the input and output vertices in columns
i
and
j
, with all other blocks propagating vertically along a single column. From
Definition

3.16
, it follows
S(B_{ij})
is the equality projector between registers
i
and
j
, and therefore

\forall i,j,\;S(B_{ij})\ket{\psi}=0\implies\ket{\psi}\in\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}.

(7.65)

Similarly, we also have that
S(P_{i})\ket{\psi}=0
, where
P_{i}
is the diagram containing singleton in the
i
th column, again all other blocks propagating vertically along a single column. In this case,
S(P_{i})
is proportional to the projector onto
\mathbf{I}\otimes\ket{+}_{i}
, and so

\forall i,\;S(P_{i})\ket{\psi}=0\implies\ket{\psi}\in\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}

(7.66)

Combining the equations above, we conclude that
\ket{\psi}\in\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}\cap\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}=\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}
.
∎

7.2.1
The Dual of a Permutation Element

Next, we prove a lemma which relates the dual of a permutation
\pi\in\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]
with the dual of the same permutation, but in the partition algebra
P_{t}(N)
:

Lemma 7.22
.

For any
\pi\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{C}[S_{t}])
, let
\pi^{*_{P_{t}(N)}}
denote the dual element of
\pi
with respect to
P_{t}(N)
, and let
\pi^{*_{\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]}}
denote the dual element of
\pi
with respect to
\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]
. For any
\sigma\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{C}[S_{t}])
,

\langle\pi^{*_{\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]}},\sigma\rangle_{2}=\langle\pi^{*_{P_{t}(N)}},\sigma\rangle_{2}

(7.67)

Equivalently, the expansions of
\pi^{*_{\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]}}
and
\pi^{*_{P_{t}(N)}}
in the basis
\mathcal{B}(P_{t}(N))
agree on all permutations
\sigma\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{C}[S_{t}])
. (Consequently, among all elements of
S_{t}
only
\pi^{-1}
appears in this expansion. )

Proof.

Since
\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]
is a group algebra,
\pi^{*_{\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]}}=\pi^{-1}/|S_{t}|
. So, it suffices to show that

\langle\pi^{*_{P_{t}(N)}},\sigma\rangle_{2}=\frac{\delta_{\pi^{-1},\sigma}}{|S_{t}|}=\frac{\delta_{\pi^{-1},\sigma}}{t!}

(7.68)

As in
Lemma

7.20
, we let
J\trianglelefteq P_{t}(N)
be the two-sided ideal spanned by all non-permutation diagrams, so that we have the canonical quotient map

\displaystyle q:P_{t}(N)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow P_{t}(N)/J,

(7.69)

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle\longmapsto a+J.

Moreover, since
P_{t}(N)
is semisimple, there exists a central idempotent
56
56

56

A central idempotent
e\in A
is an element satisfying
ea=ae
for all
a\in A
, and
e^{2}=e
. If a semisimple algebra
A
decomposed into a direct sum
I\oplus J
of two ideals, then both
I
and
J
must be of the form
eA
for some central idempotent
e
. See the discussion at the start of Chapter 22 in
[
Lam01
]
.

e
such that
[
Lam01
]
:

P_{t}(N)\simeq eP_{t}(N)\oplus J

(7.70)

Therefore,
eP_{t}(N)\simeq P_{t}(N)/J
, with one possible isomorphism given by restricting the quotient map in
Eq.

7.69
:

\displaystyle q_{e}:eP_{t}(N)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}P_{t}(N)/J,

(7.71)

\displaystyle ea

\displaystyle\longmapsto ea+J.

By definition,
q_{e}(e)=q_{e}(e\cdot\mathbf{1})=e+J
.
Moreover,
e
is a unit in
eP_{t}(N)
, so
e
also maps to
\mathbf{1}+J
, where
\mathbf{1}
is the identity permutation. Therefore,
\mathbf{1}+J=e+J
, which implies that
e=\mathbf{1}+j
for some
j\in J
.

Using that
e=\mathbf{1}+j
, we now show that
\pi^{*_{P_{t}(N)}}=\pi^{-1}e/t!
:

\displaystyle\langle D,\pi^{*_{P_{t}(N)}}\rangle_{\text{reg}_{P_{t}(N)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t!}\langle D,\pi^{-1}e\rangle_{\text{reg}_{P_{t}(N)}}

(7.72)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t!}\sum_{E\in\mathcal{B}(P_{t}(N))}\langle E,D\pi^{-1}e\cdot E\rangle_{2}

(Definition of
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\text{reg}_{P_{t}(N)}}
)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t!}\sum_{E\in\mathcal{B}(P_{t}(N))}\langle E,eD\pi^{-1}E\rangle_{2}

(
e
is central in
P_{t}(N)
)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t!}\cdot\delta_{D\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{C}[S_{t}])}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{C}[S_{t}])}\langle\sigma,eD\pi^{-1}\sigma\rangle_{2}

(
eP_{t}(N)\cap J=\{0\}
,
Eq.

7.70
)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t!}\cdot\delta_{D\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{C}[S_{t}])}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{C}[S_{t}])}\langle\sigma,(\mathbf{1}+j)D\pi^{-1}\sigma\rangle_{2}

(7.73)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t!}\cdot\delta_{D\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{C}[S_{t}])}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{C}[S_{t}])}\langle\sigma,D\pi^{-1}\sigma\rangle_{2}

(
\langle\sigma,k\rangle=0
for all
k\in J
)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t!}\cdot\delta_{D\in\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{C}[S_{t}])}\cdot(\delta_{D,\pi}\cdot t!)

(
Eq.

3.12
)

\displaystyle=\delta_{D,\pi}

(7.74)

Finally, since
\pi^{*_{P_{t}(N)}}=\pi^{-1}e/t!
,

\displaystyle\langle\pi^{*_{P_{t}(N)}},\sigma\rangle_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t!}\langle\pi^{-1}e,\sigma\rangle_{2}

(7.75)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t!}\langle\pi^{-1}(\mathbf{1}+j),\sigma\rangle_{2}

(7.76)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t!}\langle\pi^{-1},\sigma\rangle_{2}

(
\langle\sigma,k\rangle=0
for all
k\in J
)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t!}\cdot\delta_{\pi^{-1},\sigma}

(7.77)

This matches
Eq.

7.68
, concluding the proof.
∎

7.3
Additional Properties of the Update Operators

We give some useful properties of the update operators in the path-recording basis of
Section

5
.

Lemma 7.23

(
\Lambda_{S_{t}}
commutes with permutation-respecting maps on
\mathsf{R}_{X}
or
\mathsf{R}_{Y}
)
.

Let
M_{\mathsf{R}_{X}}
be a linear transformation such that
M\cdot S(\pi)=S(\pi)\cdot M
for every permutation
\pi\in S_{t}
. Then,
M_{\mathsf{R}_{X}}\otimes\mathbf{I}_{\mathsf{R}_{Y}}
commutes with
\Lambda_{S_{t}}
. This applies to
M\in\{\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}},\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}},\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}},\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}}^{\mathsf{FDist}}\}
(the latter is defined below).

The analogous statement holds for
M_{\mathsf{R}_{Y}}
.

Proof.

By
Theorem

3.11
, we know that
M
(as an operator on
(\mathbb{C}^{N})^{\otimes t}
) is in the span of
\{U^{\otimes t}\}_{U\in U(N)}
. As a result, in the Schur basis,
M
is block-diagonal with respect to the irrep
V_{U(N)}^{\lambda}\otimes V^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}
. Since
\Lambda_{S_{t}}
is block-diagonal on the
\ket{\lambda}\otimes\ket{\lambda'}
irrep spaces and acts as a scalar on each of them, we conclude that
\Lambda_{S_{t}}
commutes with
M_{\mathsf{R}_{X}}\otimes\mathbf{I}
and
\mathbf{I}\otimes M_{\mathsf{R}_{Y}}
.
∎

Lemma 7.24

(
\Lambda_{P_{t}(N)}
commutes with
\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{Y}
)
.

\Lambda_{P_{t}(N)}
commutes with
\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{Y}
.

Proof.

For
\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X}
, this follows directly from the fact that
\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}
projects, in the
(S_{N},P_{t}(N))
-Schur basis, onto irrep spaces with
|\lambda|=t
(
Lemma

7.20
).
\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{Y}
and
\Lambda_{P_{t}(N)}
act on disjoint registers.
∎

Lemma 7.25

(
\Lambda_{P_{t}(N)}
is approximately
\sqrt{\frac{t!}{N^{t}}}\mathbf{I}
on full-box irreps)
.

\displaystyle\frac{t!}{N^{t}}\left(1-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)\right)\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\preceq\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\Lambda_{P_{t}(N)}^{\dagger}\Lambda_{P_{t}(N)}\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\preceq\frac{t!}{N^{t}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)\right)\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}

(7.78)

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\frac{N^{t}}{t!}\Lambda_{P_{t}(N)}^{\dagger}\Lambda_{P_{t}(N)}\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}-\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\frac{N^{t}}{t!}\left(\sum_{\lambda\in\widehat{P}_{t}(N)}\beta_{\lambda,t}^{2}\Pi_{\lambda,t}\right)\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}-\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}\right\rVert

(7.79)

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\frac{N^{t}}{t!}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\widehat{P}_{t}(N)\\
|\lambda|=t\end{subarray}}\beta_{\lambda,t}^{2}\Pi_{\lambda,t}\right)-\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\widehat{P}_{t}(N)\\
|\lambda|=t\end{subarray}}\Pi_{\lambda,t}\right)\right\rVert

(
Lemma

7.20
)

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\widehat{P}_{t}(N)\\
|\lambda|=t\end{subarray}}\left(\frac{N^{t}}{t!}\beta_{\lambda,t}^{2}-1\right)\Pi_{\lambda,t}\right\rVert

(7.80)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\widehat{P}_{t}(N)\\
|\lambda|=t\end{subarray}}\left\lvert\frac{N^{t}}{t!}\beta_{\lambda,t}^{2}-1\right\rvert

(7.81)

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)\,.

(
Lemma

3.32
) ∎

Lemma 7.26

(
\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}
on the Distinct Nonplussed Subspace)
.

Let

\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X}:=\left(\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}}\right)_{R_{X}},\qquad\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{Y}:=\left(\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}}\right)_{R_{Y}},\qquad\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}:=\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X}\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{Y}.

Let
\Omega_{P_{t}(N)}
denote the commutant EPR projector for the partition
algebra
P_{t}(N)
, and let

\Omega_{S_{t}}:=\Omega_{\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]}

denote the commutant EPR projector for the symmetric group algebra. Then:

[\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X},\Omega_{P_{t}(N)}]=0,\qquad[\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{Y},\Omega_{P_{t}(N)}]=0,

(7.82)

[\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X},\Omega_{S_{t}}]=0,\qquad[\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{Y},\Omega_{S_{t}}]=0,

(7.83)

and

\Omega_{S_{t}}\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}=\Omega_{P_{t}(N)}\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}.

(7.84)

Proof.

We prove the three identities in order.

First, let

\Pi_{|\lambda|=t}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in\widehat{P_{t}(N)}\\
|\lambda|=t\end{subarray}}\Pi_{\lambda}

be the projector onto the full-box irreps in the
(S_{N},P_{t}(N))
-Schur decomposition
of
(\mathbb{C}^{N})^{\otimes t}
. By
Lemma

7.20
,

\Pi_{|\lambda|=t}=\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}}.

Thus

\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X}=(\Pi_{|\lambda|=t})_{\mathsf{R}_{X}},\qquad\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{Y}=(\Pi_{|\lambda|=t})_{\mathsf{R}_{Y}}.

In the double Schur basis,
\Omega_{P_{t}(N)}
is block-diagonal with respect to the irrep labels
(\lambda,\lambda^{\prime})
. Since
(\Pi_{|\lambda|=t})_{\mathsf{R}_{X}}
and
(\Pi_{|\lambda|=t})_{\mathsf{R}_{Y}}
are just projections onto a subset of these blocks, we immediately obtain that they commute with
\Omega_{P_{t}(N)}
.

Second, the symmetric-group commutant EPR projector (
Example

5.12
) has the form

\Omega_{S_{t}}=\frac{1}{t!}\sum_{\pi\in S_{t}}S(\pi)_{\mathsf{R}_{X}}\otimes S(\pi)_{\mathsf{R}_{Y}},

For every

\pi\in S_{t}
, the operator
S(\pi)
only permutes the
t
tensor factors.
The subspace
\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}
is invariant under tensor-factor permutations, and

\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}=(\mathbf{I}-\outerproduct{+}{+})^{\otimes t}

is also invariant under tensor-factor permutations. Hence

\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}=\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}\cap\mathsf{NoPlus}_{N,t}

is invariant under every
S(\pi)
. Therefore

S(\pi)\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}}=\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}}S(\pi)\qquad\forall\pi\in S_{t}.

Commuting term by term in the displayed formula for
\Omega_{S_{t}}
, we obtain

[\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X},\Omega_{S_{t}}]=0,\qquad[\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{Y},\Omega_{S_{t}}]=0.

For the third claim, we have the following calculation

\displaystyle\quad\left(\Omega^{(t)}_{P_{t}(N)}\right)_{\mathsf{R}}\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}

(7.85)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{D\in\mathcal{B}(P_{t}(N))}S(D)\otimes S(D^{*_{P_{t}(N)}})^{T}\right)\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}

(
Lemma

5.11
)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\pi\in S_{t}}S(\pi)\otimes S([\pi]^{*_{P_{t}(N)}})^{T}\right)\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}

(
Lemma

7.19
)

Next, we expand
[\pi]^{*_{P_{t}(N)}}
in the basis of
P_{t}(N)
as
\sum_{E\in\mathcal{B}(P_{t}(N))}\alpha_{\pi,E}\;E
:

\displaystyle\quad\left(\sum_{\pi\in S_{t}}S(\pi)\otimes\sum_{E\in\mathcal{B}(P_{t}(N))}\alpha_{\pi,E}\;S(E)^{T}\right)\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}

(7.86)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\pi\in S_{t}}S(\pi)\otimes\sum_{\pi^{\prime}\in\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]}\alpha_{\pi,\pi^{\prime}}\;S(\pi^{\prime})^{T}\right)\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}

(
Lemma

7.19
)

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\pi\in S_{t}}S(\pi)\otimes S([\pi]^{*_{\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]}})^{T}\right)\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}

(
Lemma

7.22
)

\displaystyle=\left(\Omega^{(t)}_{\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]}\right)_{\mathsf{R}}\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}.\qed

(7.87)

7.4
Updates in the Distinct Nonplussed Subspace

Let
V_{S_{N}}
and
V_{U(N)}
be the update operators when
G=S_{N}
or
U(N)
, respectively. In this case, the commutant algebra
\mathcal{A}_{t}
is equal to respectively either
P_{t}(N)
or
\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]
, so
Corollary

5.18
becomes

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{S_{N}}\,\cdot\,C}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\vphantom{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\right)}\Big(\Lambda_{P_{t}(N)}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}}}_{\operatorname{ReweightEnd}}\cdot\underbrace{\vphantom{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\right)}\Big(\Omega_{P_{t}(N)}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}}}_{\operatorname{Symmetrize}}\cdot\underbrace{\vphantom{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\right)}\Big(C^{\otimes t}\Big)_{\mathsf{R_{X}}}}_{C^{\otimes t}}\cdot\underbrace{\vphantom{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\right)}\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}}_{\operatorname{Append}}\cdot\underbrace{\vphantom{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\right)}\Big(\Lambda_{P_{0}(N)}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{0}}}_{\operatorname{ReweightStart}}

(7.88)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}\,\cdot\,C}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\vphantom{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\right)}\Big(\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}}}_{\operatorname{ReweightEnd}}\cdot\underbrace{\vphantom{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\right)}\Big(\Omega_{\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}}}_{\operatorname{Symmetrize}}\cdot\underbrace{\vphantom{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\right)}\Big(C^{\otimes t}\Big)_{\mathsf{R_{X}}}}_{C^{\otimes t}}\cdot\underbrace{\vphantom{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\right)}\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}}_{\operatorname{Append}}\cdot\underbrace{\vphantom{\left(\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{i}}\right)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq i}}\right)}\Big(\Lambda_{P_{0}(N)}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{0}}}_{\operatorname{ReweightStart}}

(7.89)

Let

\displaystyle\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{S_N} \,\cdot\, C}}

\displaystyle:=\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{S_{N}}\,\cdot\,C}\left(\ket{0}_{\mathsf{AB}}\otimes\ket{\emptyset}_{\mathsf{R}}\right)

(7.90)

\displaystyle\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)} \,\cdot\, C}}

\displaystyle:=\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}\,\cdot\,C}\left(\ket{0}_{\mathsf{AB}}\otimes\ket{\emptyset}_{\mathsf{R}}\right)

(7.91)

Lemma 7.27
.

We can rearrange the terms in

\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}\,\cdot\,C}
so that
C^{\otimes t}
appears at the end, as

\displaystyle\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}\,\cdot\,C}

\displaystyle=\Big(C^{\otimes t}\Big)_{\mathsf{R_{X}}}\cdot\Big(\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}}\cdot\Big(\Omega_{\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\cdot\Big(\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}[S_{0}]}^{+}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{0}}

(7.92)

\displaystyle=\Big(C^{\otimes t}\Big)_{\mathsf{R_{X}}}\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}}

(7.93)

Proof.

To see this, it suffices to show that
C^{\otimes t}
commutes with both
\Omega_{\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]}
and
\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]}
. This is clear when viewing the three operators in the Schur basis. All three are controlled by the irrep label, and
C^{\otimes t}
acts only on the unitary (group) register, while
\Omega_{\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]}
acts only on the commutant register, and
\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]}
is proportional to the identity within each block.

∎

Lemma 7.28

(Analyzing
\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\ket{0}
)
.

Let

\ket{\psi_t}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}\mathsf{R}}:=\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\ket{0}

be the unnormalized append-only state,
57
57

57

For brevity, we will write
\ket{0}
to represent
\ket{0}_{\mathsf{AB}}\ket{\emptyset}_{\mathsf{R}}
.

and let

D_{t}^{Y}:=(\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}_{N,t}})_{\mathsf{R}_{Y}}.

For every non-identity
\pi\in S_{t}
,

\bra{\psi_t}D_{t}^{Y}\Big(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi)\Big)D_{t}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}=0.

Also,

\bra{\psi_t}D_{t}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}=N^{t}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(1-\frac{i-1}{N}\right)=N^{t}\cdot\left(1-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)\right).

Proof.

We prove the claim by induction on
t
. The case
t=1
is trivial (there are no non-identity permutations and
D_{1}^{Y}=\mathbf{I}
).

For the inductive step, we write

\ket{\psi_t}=\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{t}}\cdot(A_{t})_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}\cdot\ket{\psi}_{t-1}=\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{t}}\ket{\psi_t^{\mathrm{pre}}}.

Moreover, since
D_{t}^{Y}=D_{t}^{Y}\cdot D_{t-1}^{Y}
and
D_{t-1}^{Y}
commutes with
\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{t}}
, we have that

D_{t}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}=D_{t}^{Y}\cdot\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{t}}\cdot D_{t-1}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t^{\mathrm{pre}}}.

We now write explicitly in the standard basis

D_{t-1}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t^{\mathrm{pre}}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}\\
\text{distinct }y_{1},\ldots,y_{t-1}\end{subarray}}\ket{x_t}_{\mathsf{A}}\ket{\phi_{x_{1:t}, y_{1:t-1}}}_{\mathsf{B}}\ket{x_1,\hdots x_{t-1}, y_1, \hdots, y_{t-1}}_{\mathsf{R}_{<t}}.

On this state, the effect of
D_{t}^{Y}\cdot\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{t}}
is appending with respect to an (unnormalized) EPR state over
y_{t}\notin\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{t-1}\}
(controlled on
\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{t-1}\}
), which tells us that

D_{t}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}\\
\text{distinct }y_{1},\ldots,y_{t}\end{subarray}}\ket{y_t}_{\mathsf{A}}\ket{\phi_{x_{1:t}, y_{1:t-1}}}_{\mathsf{B}}\ket{x_1,\hdots x_{t}, y_1, \hdots, y_{t}}_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq t}}.

Given that
D_{t-1}^{Y}
also commutes with
(A_{t})_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}
, this immediately tells us that

\Big|\Big|D_{t}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}\Big|\Big|^{2}=\Big(N-(t-1)\Big)\Big|\Big|D_{t-1}^{Y}\ket{\psi_{t-1}}\Big|\Big|^{2},

completing the first part of the induction. For the second part, we have that

\Big(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi)\Big)D_{t}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}\\
\text{distinct }y_{1},\ldots,y_{t}\end{subarray}}\ket{y_t}_{\mathsf{A}}\ket{\phi_{x_{1:t}, y_{1:t-1}}}_{\mathsf{B}}\ket{x_{\pi^{-1}(1)},\hdots x_{\pi^{-1}(t)}, y_{\pi^{-1}(1)}, \hdots, y_{\pi^{-1}(t)}}_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq t}}.

Now, suppose that
\pi(t)\neq t
. Then, the inner product of these two states is zero because a nonzero term in the expansion of this inner product would require terms
y_{t}=y^{\prime}_{t}
for the
\mathsf{A}
register but also
y_{\pi^{-1}(t)}=y^{\prime}_{\pi^{-1}(t)}
for the
\mathsf{R}_{Y,t}
register.

On the other hand, suppose that
\pi(t)=t
and identify
\pi
as an element of
S_{t-1}
; in particular,
S(\pi)
commutes with
\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{t}}\cdot(A_{t})_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}
. Then, we write

\bra{\psi_t}D_{t}^{Y}\Big(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi)\Big)D_{t}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}=\bra{\psi_{t}^{\mathrm{pre}}}D_{t-1}^{Y}\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{R}_{t}}^{\dagger}D_{t}^{Y}\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{R}_{t}}D_{t-1}^{Y}\Big(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi)\Big)\ket{\psi_{t}^{\mathrm{pre}}}

Next, we make use of the identity

D_{t-1}^{Y}\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{R}_{t}}^{\dagger}D_{t}^{Y}\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{R}_{t}}D_{t-1}^{Y}=(N-t+1)D_{t-1}^{Y},

which again holds because the effect of
D_{t}^{Y}\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{R}_{t}}
on the image of
D_{t-1}^{Y}
is to append an un-normalized EPR state over
y_{t}\notin\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{t-1}\}
:

D_{t}^{Y}\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A},\mathsf{R}_{t}}D_{t-1}^{Y}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots x_{t}\\
\text{distinct }y_{1},\ldots,y_{t}\end{subarray}}\outerproduct{y_t}{x_t}_{\mathsf{A}}\otimes\outerproduct{x_1, \hdots, x_t, y_1, \hdots, y_t}{x_1, \hdots, x_{t-1}, y_1, \hdots, y_{t-1}}.

The claimed identity follows from this by a direct computation.

We conclude that

\displaystyle\bra{\psi_t}D_{t}^{Y}\Big(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi)\Big)D_{t}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}

\displaystyle=(N-t+1)\bra{\psi_t^{\mathrm{pre}}}D_{t-1}^{Y}\Big(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi)\Big)\ket{\psi_t^{\mathrm{pre}}}

\displaystyle=(N-t+1)\bra{\psi_{t-1}}D_{t-1}^{Y}(A_{t}^{\dagger})_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}\Big(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi)\Big)(A_{t})_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}D_{t-1}^{Y}\ket{\psi_{t-1}}

\displaystyle=(N-t+1)\bra{\psi_{t-1}}D_{t-1}^{Y}\Big(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi)\Big)D_{t-1}^{Y}\ket{\psi_{t-1}}.

This completes the induction.
∎

In order to argue about
\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}}
, we pass to a “smaller” projection,

\displaystyle\Big(\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}}^{\mathsf{FDist}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{Y}}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\widehat{y}_{1},\ldots,\widehat{y}_{t}\\
\text{distinct and }\neq 0\end{subarray}}\outerproduct{\widehat y_1, \hdots, \widehat y_t}{\widehat y_1, \hdots, \widehat y_t}

(7.94)

\displaystyle=F_{N}^{\otimes t}\cdot(\mathbf{I}-\outerproduct{0}{0})^{\otimes t}\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\cdot(F_{N}^{\dagger})^{\otimes t},

(7.95)

where
\ket{\widehat y}\coloneqq\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}_{N}}\omega_{N}^{-yz}\ket{z}
denotes the
y
th
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
Fourier basis element of
\mathbb{C}^{N}
and
F_{N}
denotes the
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
quantum Fourier transform. In other words,
\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}}^{\mathsf{FDist}}
projects, in the
\mathbb{Z}_{N}
Fourier basis, onto distinct tuples of nonzero basis states.

Lemma 7.29

(Analyzing
\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}}^{\mathsf{FDist}}\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\ket{0}
)
.

Let

\ket{\psi_t}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}\mathsf{R}}:=\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\ket{0}

be the unnormalized append-only state, and let

D_{t}^{Y}:=(\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}}^{\mathsf{FDist}})_{\mathsf{R}_{Y}}.

For every non-identity
\pi\in S_{t}
,

\bra{\psi_t}D_{t}^{Y}\Big(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi)\Big)D_{t}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}=0.

Also,

\bra{\psi_t}D_{t}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}=N^{t}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{t}\left(1-\frac{i}{N}\right)=N^{t}\cdot\left(1-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)\right).

Proof sketch.

The proof is extremely similar to that of
Lemma

7.28
, again proceeding by induction on
t
. The case
t=1
is again trivial. For the inductive step, we use the same decomposition

\ket{\psi_t}=\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{t}}\cdot(A_{t})_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{B}}\cdot\ket{\psi}_{t-1}=\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{t}}\ket{\psi_t^{\mathrm{pre}}}

and equation

D_{t}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}=D_{t}^{Y}\cdot\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{t}}\cdot D_{t-1}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t^{\mathrm{pre}}}.

Next, we write
D_{t-1}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t^{\mathrm{pre}}}
with
\mathsf{R}_{Y}
in the
Fourier
basis:

D_{t-1}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t^{\mathrm{pre}}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}\\
\text{distinct }y_{1},\ldots,y_{t-1}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\ket{x_t}_{\mathsf{A}}\ket{\phi_{x_{1:t}, y_{1:t-1}}}_{\mathsf{B}}\ket{x_1,\hdots x_{t-1}, \widehat y_1, \hdots, \widehat y_{t-1}}_{\mathsf{R}_{<t}}.

On this state, the effect of
D_{t}^{Y}\cdot\mathsf{App}_{\mathsf{A}\mathsf{R}_{t}}
is appending with respect to an (unnormalized)
Fourier basis
EPR state

\sum_{y_{t}\notin\{0,\,y_{1},\,\ldots,\,y_{t-1}\}}\ket{\widehat{-y_t}}\ket{\widehat{y_t}}

over
y_{t}\notin\{0,\,y_{1},\,\ldots,\,y_{t-1}\}
(controlled on
\{y_{1},\ldots,y_{t-1}\}
), which tells us that

D_{t}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x_{1},\ldots,x_{t}\\
\text{distinct }y_{1},\ldots,y_{t}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\ket{\widehat{-y_t}}_{\mathsf{A}}\ket{\phi_{x_{1:t}, y_{1:t-1}}}_{\mathsf{B}}\ket{x_1,\hdots x_{t}, \widehat y_1, \hdots, \widehat y_{t}}_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq t}}.

This again completes the inductive norm calculation. In addition, an identical argument now shows that
\bra{\psi_t}D_{t}^{Y}\Big(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi)\Big)D_{t}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}=0
whenever
\pi(t)\neq t
.

On the other hand, if
\pi(t)=t
, then an entirely analogous argument shows that

\displaystyle\bra{\psi_t}D_{t}^{Y}\Big(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi)\Big)D_{t}^{Y}\ket{\psi_t}

\displaystyle=(N-t)\bra{\psi_{t-1}}D_{t-1}^{Y}\Big(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi)\Big)D_{t-1}^{Y}\ket{\psi_{t-1}}

(the only difference is that the factor
(N-t)
replaces
(N-t+1)
, from ruling out
0
in addition to
y_{1},\,\ldots,\,y_{t-1}
), completing the induction.
∎

Lemma 7.30
.

Let
\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}}
be the orthogonal projector onto the distinct nonplussed subspace (
Definition

7.5
).
Then

\displaystyle\Big|\Big|\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{Y}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)}}}\Big|\Big|^{2}\geq 1-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)

(7.96)

Proof.

By
Corollaries

7.13
and
7.15
, we have that

\Big|\Big|\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{Y}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)}}}\Big|\Big|^{2}\geq\Big|\Big|\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}}^{Y}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)}}}\Big|\Big|^{2}-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)

Thus, by the quantum union bound (
Lemma

7.8
), it suffices to prove the lemma statement for
\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}}^{Y}
rather than
\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{Y}
.

For
\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}
, we begin by writing

\displaystyle\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)}}}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\cdot\Lambda_{S_{t}}\cdot\Omega_{S_{t}}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\ket{0}

(7.97)

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{S_{t}}\cdot\Omega_{S_{t}}\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\ket{0}\,.

(by
Lemmas

7.23
and
7.26
)

Now, by
Lemma

7.28
, we have that

\displaystyle\Big\|\Omega_{S_{t}}\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\ket{0}\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(t!)^{2}}\Big\|\sum_{\pi\in S_{t}}(S(\pi)\otimes S(\pi))\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\ket{0}\Big\|^{2}

(definition of
\Omega_{S_{t}}
)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t!}\Big\|\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\ket{0}\Big\|^{2}

(by the first part of
Lemma

7.28
)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{N^{t}}{t!}\left(1-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)\right)\,.

(by the second part of
Lemma

7.28
)

Finally, we know from
Lemma

6.5
that

\Lambda_{S_{t}}^{2}\succeq\frac{t!}{N^{t}}\Big(1-O\Big(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\Big)\Big)\cdot\mathbf{I}
,
so we conclude that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)}}}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\Lambda_{S_{t}}\cdot\Omega_{S_{t}}\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\ket{0}\right\rVert^{2}

(7.98)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{t!}{N^{t}}\Big(1-O\Big(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\Big)\Big)\left\lVert\Omega_{S_{t}}\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{Dist}}^{Y}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\ket{0}\right\rVert^{2}

(7.99)

\displaystyle\geq 1-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)\,.

(7.100)

Next, to handle
\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}}
, we use the fact that

\displaystyle\Big\|\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)}}}\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\Big\|\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}}^{\mathsf{FDist}}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)}}}\Big\|^{2}.

(7.101)

An identical argument to the above then proves that

\displaystyle\Big\|\Pi_{\mathsf{NoPlus}}^{\mathsf{FDist}}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)}}}\Big\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq 1-O\Big(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\Big).

(7.102)

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 7.31
.

Let
C
be sampled from a unitary 2-design
\mathcal{D}
, and let
\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}_{N,t}}
be the orthogonal projector onto the distinct nonplussed subspace (
Definition

7.5
).
Then

\displaystyle\underset{C\leftarrow\mathcal{D}}{\mathbb{E}}\Tr[\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rvert]\geq 1-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)

(7.103)

Proof.

By a union bound, it suffices to prove the same statement for
\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X}
and
\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{Y}
separately.

For
\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X}
this follows directly from
Lemma

7.27
and
Theorem

7.7
.

For
\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{Y}
, we begin with
Lemma

7.27
, which tells us that

\displaystyle\underset{C}{\mathbb{E}}\Big|\Big|\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{Y}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)}\cdot C}}\Big|\Big|^{2}

\displaystyle=\underset{C}{\mathbb{E}}\Big|\Big|\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{Y}C^{\otimes t}_{\mathsf{R}_{X}}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)}}}\Big|\Big|^{2}

(7.104)

\displaystyle=\underset{C}{\mathbb{E}}\Big|\Big|C^{\otimes t}_{\mathsf{R}_{X}}\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{Y}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)}}}\Big|\Big|^{2}

(7.105)

\displaystyle=\Big|\Big|\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{Y}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)}}}\Big|\Big|^{2},

(7.106)

as
C^{\otimes t}_{\mathsf{R}_{X}}
is unitary and commutes with
\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{Y}
. The conclusion now follows from
Lemma

7.30
.
∎

Lemma 7.32
.

Let
C
be sampled from a unitary 2-design
\mathcal{D}
, and let
\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}
be the distinct nonplussed subspace projector.
Then

\displaystyle\underset{C\leftarrow\mathcal{D}}{\mathbb{E}}\Tr[\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{S_{N}}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{S_{N}}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rvert]\geq 1-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)

(7.107)

Proof.

\displaystyle\underset{C}{\mathbb{E}}\left\lVert\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{S_N} \,\cdot\, C}}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\underset{C}{\mathbb{E}}\left\lVert\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\cdot\Lambda_{P_{t}(N)}\cdot\Omega_{P_{t}(N)}\cdot C^{\otimes t}_{\mathsf{R_{X}}}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\ket{0}\right\rVert^{2}

(7.108)

\displaystyle=\underset{C}{\mathbb{E}}\left\lVert\Lambda_{P_{t}(N)}\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\cdot\Omega_{P_{t}(N)}\cdot C^{\otimes t}_{\mathsf{R_{X}}}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\ket{0}\right\rVert^{2}

(
Lemma

7.24
)

\displaystyle=\underset{C}{\mathbb{E}}\left\lVert\Lambda_{P_{t}(N)}\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\cdot\Omega_{S_{t}}\cdot C^{\otimes t}_{\mathsf{R_{X}}}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\ket{0}\right\rVert^{2}

(
Lemma

7.26
)

\displaystyle\geq\underset{C}{\mathbb{E}}\left\lVert\Lambda_{S_{t}}\cdot\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\cdot\Omega_{S_{t}}\cdot C^{\otimes t}_{\mathsf{R_{X}}}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\ket{0}\right\rVert^{2}\left(1-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)\right)

(
Lemmas

6.5
and
7.25
)

\displaystyle=\underset{C}{\mathbb{E}}\left\lVert\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\cdot\Lambda_{S_{t}}\cdot\Omega_{S_{t}}\cdot C^{\otimes t}_{\mathsf{R_{X}}}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\ket{0}\right\rVert^{2}\left(1-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)\right)

(
Lemma

7.23
)

\displaystyle=\underset{C}{\mathbb{E}}\left\lVert\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)} \,\cdot\, C}}\right\rVert^{2}\left(1-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)\right)

(7.109)

\displaystyle\geq 1-O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)

(
Lemma

7.31
) ∎

Corollary 7.33
.

Using
Lemmas

7.31
,
7.32
and
6.1
we get the following trace distance bounds:

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}^{\phantom{R}}\left[\underset{C\leftarrow\mathcal{D}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rvert\right]-\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}^{\phantom{R}}\left[\underset{C\leftarrow\mathcal{D}}{\mathbb{E}}\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rvert\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\right]\right\rVert_{1}\leq O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)

(7.110)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}^{\phantom{R}}\left[\underset{C\leftarrow\mathcal{D}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{S_{N}}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{S_{N}}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rvert\right]-\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}^{\phantom{R}}\left[\underset{C\leftarrow\mathcal{D}}{\mathbb{E}}\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{S_{N}}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{S_{N}}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rvert\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\right]\right\rVert_{1}\leq O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)

(7.111)

7.5
Putting it Together

Finally, we compare the cases
G=U(N)
and
G=S_{N}
of
Corollary

5.18
, and show that the
G
-dependent steps are close in expectation whenever
C
is sampled from a unitary
2
-design. This implies that queries to
PC
for a random permutation matrix
P\in S_{N}
are (nearly) indistinguishable from queries to
UC=U^{\prime}
for a Haar random unitary
U\in U(N)
, leading to the conclusion that
PC
is a pseudorandom unitary for any pseudorandom permutation
P
and any unitary
2
-design
C
.

Theorem 7.34
.

\displaystyle\left\lVert\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}P\leftarrow S_{N}\\
C\leftarrow\mathcal{D}\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\outerproduct*{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{P \,\cdot\, C}}{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{P \,\cdot\, C}}\Big]-\underset{U\leftarrow U(N)}{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\outerproduct*{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{U}}{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{U}}\Big]\right\rVert_{1}\leq O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)

(7.112)

Proof.

By the correctness of the path-recording oracles, we know that for every
C
,

\displaystyle\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}^{\phantom{R}}\!\!\left[\;\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rvert\;\right]

\displaystyle=\underset{U\leftarrow U(N)}{\mathbb{E}}\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{U\cdot C}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{U\cdot C}}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\underset{U\leftarrow U(N)}{\mathbb{E}}\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{U}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{U}}\right\rvert

(7.113)

and

\displaystyle\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}^{\phantom{R}}\!\!\left[\;\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{S_{N}}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{S_{N}}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rvert\;\right]

\displaystyle=\underset{P\leftarrow S_{N}}{\mathbb{E}}\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{P\cdot C}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{P\cdot C}}\right\rvert.

(7.114)

Therefore, by
Corollary

7.33
, it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}^{\phantom{R}}\!\!\left[\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}C\leftarrow\mathcal{D}\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{S_{N}}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{S_{N}}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rvert\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\right]\!-\Tr_{\mathsf{R}}^{\phantom{R}}\!\!\left[\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}C\leftarrow\mathcal{D}\end{subarray}}{\mathbb{E}}\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rvert\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\right]\right\rVert_{1}\leq O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)

(7.115)

In fact, we prove the following stronger statement: for every fixed
C
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{S_{N}}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{S_{N}}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rvert\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}-\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\left\lvert{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle{\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{V_{U(N)}\,\cdot\,C}}\right\rvert\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\right\rVert_{1}\leq O\left(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\right)

(7.116)

To see this, we make use of the standard manipulation

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\outerproduct*{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{S_N} \,\cdot\, C}}{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{S_N} \,\cdot\, C}}\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}-\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\outerproduct*{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)} \,\cdot\, C}}{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)} \,\cdot\, C}}\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\right\rVert_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\cdot\left\lVert\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{S_N} \,\cdot\, C}}-\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)} \,\cdot\, C}}\right\rVert

(7.117)

Now, given that our starting state is empty, we can write (for both
V=V_{U(N)}
and
V=V_{S_{N}}
)

\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V \cdot C}}=\Big(\Lambda_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq t}}\cdot\Big(\Omega_{\mathcal{A}_{t}}\Big)_{\mathsf{R}_{\leq t}}\cdot\Big(C^{\otimes t}\Big)_{\mathsf{R_{X}}}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\cdot\ket{0}.

(7.118)

Letting
\ket{\psi}=\Big(C^{\otimes t}\Big)_{\mathsf{R_{X}}}\cdot\mathsf{Adv}_{t}^{\operatorname{App}}\cdot\ket{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{S_N} \,\cdot\, C}}-\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)} \,\cdot\, C}}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\Big(\Lambda_{P_{t}(N)}\Omega_{P_{t}(N)}-\Lambda_{S_{t}}\Omega_{S_{t}}\Big)\ket{\psi}\right\rVert

(7.119)

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\Big(\Lambda_{P_{t}(N)}\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\Omega_{P_{t}(N)}-\Lambda_{S_{t}}\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\Omega_{S_{t}}\Big)\ket{\psi}\right\rVert

(7.120)

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\Big(\Lambda_{P_{t}(N)}-\Lambda_{S_{t}}\Big)\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\Omega_{S_{t}}\ket{\psi}\right\rVert

(7.121)

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\Big(\Lambda_{P_{t}(N)}-\Lambda_{S_{t}}\Big)\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\Lambda_{S_{t}}^{+}\ket{\mathsf{Adv}_t^{V_{U(N)} \,\cdot\, C}}\right\rVert

(7.122)

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\Big(\Lambda_{P_{t}(N)}-\Lambda_{S_{t}}\Big)\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\right\rVert\cdot\left\lVert\Lambda_{S_{t}}^{+}\right\rVert.

(7.123)

By
Lemmas

6.5
and
7.25
, on the full-box/DNP blocks the squared reweighting matrices

\Lambda_{P_{t}(N)}^{\dagger}\Lambda_{P_{t}(N)}
and
\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]}^{\dagger}\Lambda_{\mathbb{C}[S_{t}]}
differ by a multiplicative

1+O(t^{2}/N)
. Taking square roots preserves this error up to constants, so it follows that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Big(\Lambda_{P_{t}(N)}-\Lambda_{S_{t}}\Big)\Pi_{\mathsf{DNP}}^{X,Y}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{t!}{N^{t}}}\cdot O\Big(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\Big).

(7.124)

Similarly, by
Lemma

6.5
we have that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Lambda_{S_{t}}^{+}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{N^{t}}{t!}}\cdot\Big(1+O\Big(\frac{t^{2}}{N}\Big)\Big).

(7.125)

This completes the proof of the theorem.
∎

The following corollary formalizes
Theorems

1.6
and
1.7
to conclude that
P_{k}\cdot C
is either a pseudorandom unitary or a
t
-design, if
P_{k}
is instantiated with respectively either a pseudorandom permutation or a
2t
-wise independent permutation.

Corollary 7.35
.

Suppose that
P_{k}
is a
(t,\varepsilon)
-secure in-place post-quantum PRP, meaning that for all polynomial-time
t
-query quantum algorithms
\mathsf{Adv}
, we have that
\mathsf{Adv}^{P_{k}}\approx_{\varepsilon}\mathsf{Adv}^{P}
for a uniformly random permutation unitary
P
. Moreover, let
C\leftarrow\mathcal{D}
be a unitary sampled from an exact unitary
2
-design.
Then,
P_{k}\cdot C
is a
(t,\varepsilon+O(t^{2}/N))
-secure PRU.
Moreover, if
P_{k}
is secure against inefficient
\mathsf{Adv}
, so is
P_{k}\cdot C
.

Proof.

This immediately follows from
Theorem

7.34
and a hybrid argument switching
P_{k}
to a uniformly random
P
.
∎
